Good moduli for moduli of objects
Enrico Lampetti

Abstract
. We construct good moduli spaces from moduli of objects in the sense of Toën-Vaquié. As an application, we construct good moduli spaces for perverse sheaves.



1.
Introduction

Good moduli spaces for algebraic stacks were introduced by Alper in
[
Alp13
]
and have played a prominent role in moduli theory as generalizations of Mumford’s good GIT quotients
[
MFK94
]
and Abramovich-Olsson-Vistoli’s tame stacks
[
AOV08
]
.
For the sake of this introduction, recall that a good moduli space for an algebraic stack
\mathcal{X}
is a qcqs morphism
q\colon\mathcal{X}\to X
to an algebraic space such that the pushforward along
q
is exact on quasi-coherent sheaves and such that the canonical morphism
\mathcal{O}_{X}\to q_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
is an equivalence.
If a good moduli space
q\colon\mathcal{X}\to X
exists, then it is unique, as
q
is universal for maps from
\mathcal{X}
to algebraic spaces (
[
Alp13
, Theorem 6.6]
). In a similar way that the Keel-Mori Theorem
[
KM97
]
provides an intrinsic way to show that a Deligne-Mumford stack admits a coarse moduli space, a fundamental result of Alper-Halpern Leistner-Heinloth
[
AHLH23
, Theorem A]
provides an intrinsic way to show that an algebraic stack admits a good moduli space.
The existence of a good moduli space for an algebraic stack
\mathcal{X}
has many pleasant consequences.
It allows for example to give a local presentation of
\mathcal{X}
by quotient stacks and prove the compact generation of
\QCoh(\mathcal{X})
(
[
AHR25
]
).
Also, the existence of good moduli spaces can be exploited to construct BPS Lie algebras and unlock the study of the cohomology of
\mathcal{X}
via cohomological Hall algebras, see e.g.
[
DHM22
,
Dav24
,
BDN+25
,
Hen24
]
.

As a consequence of
[
AHLH23
, Theorem A]
, given a nice enough cocomplete abelian category
\mathcal{C}
, the authors construct a good moduli space for Artin-Zhang’s moduli of objects
[
AZ01
]
parametrizing compact objects in
\mathcal{C}
.
This good moduli space has been further studied by Fernandez Herrero-Lennen-Makarova in
[
FHLM25
]
and, when it exists, is necessarily
proper
by
[
AHLH23
, Theorem 7.23]
.
Still many interesting moduli encountered in algebraic geometry are not proper.
As an example, for a complex algebraic variety
X
one can show by hand that the algebraic stack
\mathbf{Loc}(X)
parametrizing local systems with finite dimensional stalks on
X
admits a good moduli space, that is the character variety of
X
, which is non proper in general.
In particular,
\mathbf{Loc}(X)
does not fit in the framework of
[
AZ01
]
.
The point is that the compact objects of the cocomplete category
\Loc(X;\Mod_{\mathbb{C}})
do not need to have finite dimensional stalks, as the following example shows :

Example 1.0.1
.

Consider the local system
L
on
X=\mathbb{G}_{m}
corresponding to the representation of
\pi_{1}(X)\simeq\mathbb{Z}
defined by
\bigoplus_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{C}
with
\mathbb{Z}
-action given by translation.
The object
L
is compact in
\Loc(X;\Mod_{\mathbb{C}})
as it corepresents the stalk functor, but its stalks are not finite dimensional.

In particular, the above example shows that to single-out local systems with finite dimensional stalks, one needs to impose a condition stronger than compactness.
As proved in
[
PT22
,
HPT25
]
, it turns out that local systems with finite dimensional stalks correspond to
pseudo-perfect
objects of
\Loc(X;\Mod_{\mathbb{C}})
in the sense of Toën-Vaquié
[
TV07
]
.
Thus, to construct a good moduli for
\mathbf{Loc}(X)
in an intrinsic way as in
[
AHLH23
]
, one is naturally led to replace Artin-Zhang moduli by Toën-Vaquié moduli of objects parametrizing pseudo-perfect objects.

Note that pseudo-perfect objects require
\infty
-categories to make sense
[
TV05
,
TV08
,
Lur09
,
Lur17
]
.
Furthermore in Toën-Vaquié’s setting,
\mathcal{C}
is not an abelian category anymore, but a triangulated category (actually its
\infty
-categorically avatar, that is a stable
\infty
-category).
Then, the original abelian category of interest appears as the heart
\mathcal{C}^{\heartsuit}
of a
t
-structure
\tau
on
\mathcal{C}
.
The next issue is then to extract from Toën-Vaquié moduli of objects
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}
a substack parametrizing objects in
\mathcal{C}^{\heartsuit}
.
Such problem has already been encountered by Lieblich
[
Lie06
]
for the stack of perfect complexes and led to the notion of
\tau
-flat
objects in
\mathcal{C}
.
Following
[
AP06
,
Pol07
,
BLM+21
,
Por25
]
, we define

\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}

as the substack parametrizing
\tau
-flat objects in
\mathcal{C}
.
The stack
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
is the central player of this paper. Before moving to our main results, observe that working at this level of generality gives access to new moduli spaces that cannot be constructed via classical algebraic geometry.
For example, the derived moduli of perverse sheaves from
[
PT22
,
HPT24
,
HPT25
]
provides a far reaching generalization of the character stack and fits into Toën-Vaquié’s framework.

Let us now introduce the main result of this paper.
Let
k
be a noetherian ring of characteristic
0
and
\mathcal{C}
be a finite type
k
-linear
\infty
-category equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
(
Definition

2.2.3
).
Let
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}
be Toën-Vaquié moduli of objects parametrizing pseudo-perfect objects in
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}
be the substack parametrizing
\tau
-flat pseudo-perfect objects in
\mathcal{C}
(
Definition

2.2.5
). Under suitable natural assumptions on
\tau
called openness of flatness (
Definition

2.2.23
), the substack

\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
is an open substack of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}
. The last input necessary to construct good moduli spaces is a technical assumption on
\tau
loosely stated as

Assumption 1.0.2
.

For any DVR
R
over
k
, for every pseudo-perfect object
F
with coefficients in
R
and lying in
\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
, the subobjects of
F
are also pseudo-perfect.

1.0.2
is satisfied in many cases of interest.

Example 1.0.3
.

For local systems on a complex algebraic variety, pseudo-perfect objects over
R
correspond to local systems of
R
-modules with finitely generated stalks.
Then
1.0.2
is immediately satisfied.

Example 1.0.4
.

For perverse sheaves, pseudo-perfect objects over
R
correspond to perverse sheaves of
R
-modules with perfect stalks.
In that case,
1.0.2
is satisfied in virtue of
Proposition

4.1.8
.

Example 1.0.5
.

Let
X
be a smooth scheme over
k
.
For coherent sheaves, a theorem of Ben Zvi-Nadler-Preygel
[
BZNP17
, Theorem 3.0.2]
describes the pseudo-perfect objects over
R
as the coherent sheaves over
X_{R}
with proper support.
In that case,
1.0.2
is thus obvious.

In the above setup, let
\Spec(\kappa)\to\Spec(k)
be a closed point with
\kappa
algebraically closed.
Our main result is the following

Theorem 1.0.6

(
Theorem

3.3.2
)
.

Every closed quasi-compact substack
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
admits a separated good moduli space
X
. Moreover, the
\kappa
-points of
X
parametrize pseudo-perfect semisimple objects of
\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}^{\heartsuit}
lying over
\mathcal{X}
.

Let us point out that a notion of derived good moduli spaces has been introduced by Ahlqvist-Hekking-Pernice-Savvas in
[
AHPS23
]
and that often derived enhancement of a good moduli space come for free thanks to
[
AHPS23
, Theorem 2.12]
.

The main tool for constructing good moduli spaces is
[
AHLH23
, Theorem A]
.
It states that an algebraic stack of finite presentation over a noetherian ring of characteristic zero admits a separated good moduli space if and only if it is "
\Theta
-reductive" (
Definition

2.4.3
) and "
\mathrm{S}
-complete" (
Definition

2.4.4
).
These two properties are of valuative nature and involve the algebraic stacks

\Theta_{R}\coloneqq\left[{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{A_{1,R}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{A_{1,R}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}$}}{\raisebox{2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{A_{1,R}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{A_{1,R}}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.6994pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}$}}}\right],\qquad\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\coloneqq\left[{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\scriptstyle{\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.6994pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}$}}}\right],

for
R
a DVR and
\pi\in R
a uniformizer.
For moduli of objects one can explicitly describe
\Theta
-reductiveness and
S
-completeness (
Proposition

3.1.11
and
Proposition

3.2.6
).
The explicit description of
\Theta
-reductiveness repose on the work Moulinos
[
Mou21
]
, whose main result implies the existence of a symmetric monoidal equivalence

\QCoh(\Theta_{R})\simeq\Rep(\mathbb{Z};\Mod_{R}),

where the category on the right hand side is the category of filtered
R
-modules.
The explicit description of
\mathrm{S}
-completeness repose on Appendix
A
, where we use similar techniques to
[
Mou21
]
. For
R
a DVR with uniformizer
\pi
, our main results is the existence of a symmetric monoidal equivalence

\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})\simeq\Mod_{R[s,t]/(st-\pi)}(\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds};\Mod_{R})),

where the category on the right hand side is the category of graded
R
-modules equipped with an action of
s
of weight 1 and an action of
t
of weight
-1
, subjects to the relations
st=\pi=ts
.

Theorem

1.0.6
reduces the existence of good moduli spaces from moduli of objects to the existence of closed quasi-compact substacks
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
.
We exhibit a large class of such in
Section

3.3.1
and we give a hint of the construction below.
For this purpose, let
\Spec(\kappa)\to\Spec(k)
be a closed point with
\kappa
algebraically closed, let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\underline{F}\subset\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}^{\heartsuit}
be a finite set of pseudo-perfect semisimple objects over
\kappa
.
Then
3.3.11
shows that there exists a quasi-compact closed substack

\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\underline{F},N}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}

whose closed
\kappa
-points are in bijection with extension of length at most
N
of objects in
\underline{F}
.
Hence
Theorem

1.0.6
gives

Theorem 1.0.7

(
Theorem

3.3.14
)
.

In the setting of
Theorem

1.0.6
, the algebraic stack
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\underline{F},N}
admits a separated good moduli space
\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\underline{F},N}
. Moreover, the
\kappa
-points of
\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\underline{F},N}
are in bijection with direct sum of at most
N
objects in
\underline{F}
, possibly with repetitions.

Let us now describe particular instances of
Theorem

1.0.6
.

1.1.
Perverse sheaves

The role of perverse sheaves is central in the study of singularities and they naturally generalize local system. Moreover they are linked to the study of differential equation with regular singularities (i.e., simple poles) via the Riemann-Hilbert correspondence (
[
Kas84
,
Meb84
]
).
Perverse sheaves originated form the theory of
\mathcal{D}
-modules via the Riemann-Hilbert correspondence as the shifted solution complex and the shifted de Rham complex of a holonomic
\mathcal{D}
-module on a complex manifold (
[
Kas84
,
KS90
]
).
An axiomatic background has been introduced in the influential
[
BBDG18
]
.
For a stratified space
(X,P)
, let
\mathfrak{p}\colon P\to\mathbb{Z}
be a function, called
perversity
.
We can attach to
\mathfrak{p}
a perverse
t
-structure
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau
on the derived category of constructible sheaves on
X
and the category of perverse sheaves is defined as its heart.
The derived stack
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
of perverse sheaves on
(X,P)
with perversity
\mathfrak{p}
has been constructed in
[
PT22
,
HPT24
,
HPT25
]
and fits in our framework.
We denote by
t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
the truncation of
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
, which is an algebraic stack locally of finite presentation over
k
.

We will provide the existence of good moduli spaces for perverse sheaves for a large class of stratified space, including real algebraic varieties equipped and compact analytic varieties equipped with Whitney stratifications.
The key result here is
Proposition

4.1.8
:
it shows that a subobject of a perverse sheaf with perfect stalks also has perfect stalks as long as we take coefficients in
\Mod_{R}
for
R
a regular noetherian ring.
As already discussed in
Example

1.0.3
, this is precisely the translation of
3.0.1
to the case of perverse sheaves.
Hence
Theorem

1.0.6
and
Theorem

1.0.7
apply.

If
(X,P)
is a Whitney stratified manifold (
Definition

4.0.18
) and
\mathfrak{p}\colon P\to\mathbb{Z}
the middle perversity (
Definition

4.2.8
), we can prove the existence of a good moduli space for the entire algebraic stack of perverse sheaves, hence generalizing
Theorem

1.0.6
.
More precisely, in the above setting and for
\Spec(\kappa)\to\Spec(k)
a closed point with
\kappa
algebraically closed, our main result is the following

Theorem 1.1.1

(
Theorem

4.2.32
)
.

The algebraic stack
t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
admits a separated good moduli space
t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P}(X)
.
Moreover, the
\kappa
-points of
t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P}(X)
parametrize semisimple perverse sheaves with perfect stalks.
Furthermore,
t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P}(X)
admits a natural derived enhancement
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P}(X)
which is a derived good moduli space for
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
.

1.2.
Constructible sheaves and Stokes data

Other possible generalizations for local systems are constructible sheaves and Stokes data.
Although the corresponding moduli fit in our framework, after writing this paper we realized that these moduli satisfy a form of
Hartogs’ principle

[
AHLH23
, Remark 3.51]
, that readily implies
\Theta
-reductiveness and
S
-completeness.
Moreover, they admit natural quasi-compact open and closed substacks.
This makes the construction of the good moduli spaces easier and it will appear in a companion paper
[
Lam25
]
.
However, in order to characterize closed points of the moduli of constructible sheaves and of Stokes data as well as points of their respective good moduli spaces, we need all the strength of the results contained in the present paper.

1.3.
Linear overview

In
Section

2
we recall the definitions and results about moduli of objects in the sense of Toën-Vaquié and of good moduli spaces that we will need in the rest of the paper.

In
Section

3
we prove the existence of good moduli spaces for moduli of objects of a finite type category
\mathcal{C}
equipped with a
t
-structure satisfying
3.0.1
.
More precisely, in
Section

3.1
we prove that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
is
\Theta
-reductive, while in
Section

3.2
we prove that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
is
\mathrm{S}
-complete.
As a corollary, we obtain a characterization of closed points of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
as those whose corresponding object in
\mathcal{C}^{\heartsuit}
is semisimple (
Corollary

3.2.11
).
We then move on giving the main result of this paper, namely the existence of good moduli spaces for quasi-compact closed substacks of moduli of objects (
Theorem

3.3.2
).
We give applications of the above result in
Theorem

3.3.14
.

In
Section

4
we specialize our discussion to the case of perverse sheaves.
More precisely, we show in
Proposition

4.1.8
that the perverse
t
-structure satisfy
3.0.1
(see
Example

4.0.11
).
In

In Appendix
A
we recall results on the derived category
\QCoh(\Theta_{R})
from
[
Mou21
]
and we provide an explicit description of the derived category
\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})
. These results are used in
Section

3
.

1.4.
Notation

We introduce the following running notations.

•

We fix
k
a discrete noetherian ring of characteristic
0
, that is, a discrete noetherian ring containing
\mathbb{Q}
;

•

\mathrm{Grpd}
is the category of groupoids;

•

\mathrm{Spc}
is the
\infty
-category of spaces;

•

\mathrm{Sp}
is the
\infty
-category of spectra;

•

\Cat_{\infty}
is the
\infty
-category of
\infty
-categories;

•

(-)^{\omega}\colon\Cat_{\infty}\to\Cat_{\infty}
is the functor that assign to an
\infty
-category its full subcategory spanned compact objects;

•

(-)^{\simeq}\colon\Cat_{\infty}\to\Spc
is the functor that assign to an
\infty
-category its maximal
\infty
-sub-grupoid;

•

\Pr^{\mathrm{L}}_{k}
is the
\infty
-category of presentable
k
-linear
\infty
-categories with left adjoints functors;

•

\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
is the
\infty
-category of presentable compactly generated
k
-linear
\infty
-categories with left adjoints functors;

•

\Aff_{k}
is the opposite of the category of
k
-algebras;

•

\dAff_{k}
is the opposite of the
\infty
-categories of simplicial
k
-algebras;

•

the category of stacks over
k
is the full subcategory

\mathrm{St}_{k}\subset\Fun(\Aff_{k},\mathrm{Grpd})

spanned by stacks over
k
;

•

the
\infty
-category of derived stacks over
k
is the full subcategory

\dSt_{k}\subset\Fun(\dAff_{k},\mathrm{Spc})

spanned by derived stacks over
k
;

•

denote by
\tau_{\geq 1}:\mathrm{Spc}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
the functor that assign to a space its homotopy category, which is a groupoid.
Denote by
i:\Aff_{k}\to\dAff_{k}
the canonical inclusion.
The composition

\Fun(\dAff_{k},\mathrm{Spc})\xrightarrow{-\circ i}\Fun(\Aff_{k},\mathrm{Spc})\xrightarrow{\tau_{\geq 1}\circ-}\Fun(\Aff_{k},\mathrm{Grpd})

restricts to a functor
t_{0}\colon\dSt_{k}\to\St_{k}
.
We will refer to
t_{0}
as the truncation functor.

2.
Moduli of objects and
t
-structures

In this section we recall the main definitions, constructions and results needed in the rest of the paper.

2.1.
Families of objects

In this paragraph we recall properties of the tensor product of presentable
\infty
-categories, which is used to define families of objects.
We also discuss some finiteness properties one can impose on compactly generated presentable categories.

Recollection 2.1.1

(
[
Lur17
, Section 4.8]
)
.

For
\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{k}
, we can consider their tensor product

\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}\mathcal{D}\simeq\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{R}}_{k}(\mathcal{C}^{op},\mathcal{D})

where the right hand side is the
\infty
-category of
k
-linear functors commuting with limits.
This tensor product endows
\Pr^{\mathrm{L}}_{k}
with a symmetric monoidal structure which restricts to
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
.
When
\mathcal{C}
is compactly generated, there is a canonical equivalence

\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}\mathcal{D}\simeq\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}((\mathcal{C}^{\omega})^{op},\mathcal{D})

where the right hand side is the
\infty
-category of exact
k
-linear functors.

Remark 2.1.2
.

Let
\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D},\mathcal{E}\in\Pr_{k}^{\mathrm{L}}
.
Then an adjunction

f\colon\mathcal{D}\leftrightarrows\mathcal{E}\colon g

induces an adjunction

\Id_{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{k}f\colon\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}\mathcal{D}\leftrightarrows\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}\mathcal{E}\colon\Id_{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{k}g.

The functor
\Id_{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{k}g
corresponds to

g\circ-\colon\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{R}}_{k}(\mathcal{C}^{op},\mathcal{E})\to C\otimes_{k}\mathcal{E}\simeq\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{R}}_{k}(\mathcal{C}^{op},\mathcal{D})

under the equivalences of
2.1.1
.
Its left adjoint
\Id_{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{k}f
has no easy description in general.
Notice indeed that post-composition with
f
do not preserve functors that commutes with limits.
Nevertheless, if
\mathcal{C}
is compactly generated one can see that the adjunction
\Id_{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{k}f\dashv\Id_{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{k}g
corresponds to

f\circ-\colon\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}((\mathcal{C}^{\omega})^{op},\mathcal{D})\leftrightarrows\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}((\mathcal{C}^{\omega})^{op},\mathcal{D})\colon g\circ-

under the equivalences of
2.1.1
.

Notation 2.1.3
.

In the setting of
Remark

2.1.2
, we denote the functors
\Id_{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{k}f,\Id_{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{k}g
respectively by
f_{\mathcal{C}},g_{\mathcal{C}}
.

In the stable setting, the following result is a reformulation of
[
SS03
, Lemma 2.2.1]
.

Lemma 2.1.4

(
[
AG14
, Lemma 2.4]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}
stable and
X\subset\mathcal{E}^{\omega}
a set of compact objects.
Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

\forall y\in\mathcal{E}
,

\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{E}}(x,y)\simeq*\in\mathrm{Sp}\ \ \forall x\in X\Rightarrow y\simeq 0;

(2)

the
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}^{\omega}
is the smallest full subcategory of
\mathcal{E}
containing
X
and stable under finite colimits, shifts and retracts.

Definition 2.1.5
.

Let
\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}
stable and
X\subset\mathcal{E}^{\omega}
a set of compact objects.
We say that
X
is a set of compact generators if it satisfies the equivalent conditions of
Lemma

2.1.4
.

Example 2.1.6
.

Let
A\in\mathrm{Alg}_{k}
.
Then
A
is a compact generator of
\Mod_{A}
in the sense of
Definition

2.1.5
.

Definition 2.1.7
.

For
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L}}_{k}
and
X\in\dSt_{k}
, define the
\infty
-category of
X
-families of objects in
\mathcal{C}
as

\mathcal{C}_{X}\coloneqq\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}\QCoh(X).

Notation 2.1.8
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\Pr^{\mathrm{L}}_{k}
and
S=\Spec(A)\in\dAff_{k}
.
The
\infty
-category of
S
-family of objects in
\mathcal{C}
will be denoted equivalently by
\mathcal{C}_{S}
or by
\mathcal{C}_{A}
.

Definition 2.1.9
.

For
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
and
X\in\dSt_{k}
, define the
\infty
-category of pseudo-perfect families over
X
in
\mathcal{C}
as the full subcategory

\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{st}}((C^{\omega})^{op},\Perf(X))\subset\mathcal{C}_{X}

spanned by functors valued in
\Perf(X)
.

In order to introduce finiteness conditions on presentable categories, let us give the following:

Lemma 2.1.10

(
[
Lur18
, Theorem D.7.0.7]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
.
Then
\mathcal{C}
is dualizable in
\Pr_{k}^{\mathrm{L}}
with dual given by
\mathcal{C}^{\vee}\simeq\mathrm{Ind}((\mathcal{C}^{\omega})^{op})
.
In particular, there are up to a contractible space of choices unique maps in
\Pr^{\mathrm{L}}_{k}

\mathrm{ev}_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathcal{C}^{\vee}\otimes_{k}\mathcal{C}\to\Mod_{k}

\mathrm{coev}_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\Mod_{k}\to\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}\mathcal{C}^{\vee}

satisfying the triangular identities.

We will need the following finiteness condition:

Definition 2.1.11
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
.
We say that
\mathcal{C}
is

(1)

smooth if
\ev_{\mathcal{C}}
preserves compact objects;

(2)

proper if
\coev_{\mathcal{C}}
preserve compact objects;

(3)

of finite type if it is a compact object of
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
.

The next result compares the different finiteness conditions of
Definition

2.1.11
.

Proposition 2.1.12

(
[
Por25
, Proposition I.4.16]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
.
Then:

(1)

if
\mathcal{C}
is of finite type, it is smooth;

(2)

if
\mathcal{C}
is smooth and proper, it is of finite type.

Moreover, if
\mathcal{C}
is smooth then it admits a compact generator in the sense of
Definition

2.1.5
.

Lemma 2.1.13
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
.
The following are equivalent:

(1)

\mathcal{C}
is smooth;

(2)

there exists an equivalence
\mathcal{C}\simeq\LMod_{A}
with
A\in\mathrm{Alg}_{k}
smooth in the sense of
[
Lur17
, Definition 4.6.4.13]
.

This follows by combining
[
Lur18
, Proposition 11.3.2.4]
with
[
Lur17
, Remark 4.2.1.37 & Remark 4.6.4.15]
.
∎

Remark 2.1.14
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be smooth and
E\in\mathcal{C}
be a compact generator.
Lurie shows in the proof of
[
Lur18
, Proposition 11.3.2.4]
that
A\coloneqq\End(E)\in\mathrm{Alg}_{k}
is smooth and
\mathcal{C}\simeq\LMod_{A^{rev}}
, where
A^{rev}
is the opposite algebra of
A
.

Example 2.1.15
.

In the setting of
Remark

2.1.14
and for
X\in\dSt_{k}
, it follows from

[
Lur17
, Theorem 4.8.5.16-(4) & Remark 4.2.1.37]
that we have an equivalence

\mathcal{C}_{X}\simeq\LMod_{A}(\Mod_{k})\otimes_{k}\LMod_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\QCoh(X))\simeq\LMod_{A\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\QCoh(X)).

Corollary 2.1.16
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
be a smooth category and
E\in\mathcal{C}
be a compact generator.
Let
X
be a derived stack over
k
.
Then
F\in\mathcal{C}_{X}
is pseudo-perfect if and only if
F(E)\in\Perf(X)
.

Since
E
generates
\mathcal{C}^{\omega}
under shifts, finite colimits and retracts and
\Perf(X)
is closed in
\QCoh(X)
under shifts finite (co)limits and retracts, the result follows.
∎

Lemma 2.1.17

(
[
Por25
, Corollary I.5.7]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
and let
\Spec(A)\in\dAff_{k}
.

(1)

If
\mathcal{C}
is smooth, then any pseudo-perfect family in
\mathcal{C}_{A}
is compact.

(2)

If
\mathcal{C}
is proper, then any compact object in
\mathcal{C}_{A}
is pseudo-perfect.

Remark 2.1.18
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
smooth and
\Spec(B)\in\dAff_{k}
.
Let
E
be a compact generator of
\mathcal{C}
(
Proposition

2.1.12
).
Then
E\otimes_{k}A
is a compact generator of
\mathcal{C}_{A}
.
This follows by the functor
\mathrm{Forget}\colon\mathcal{C}_{A}\to\mathcal{C}
being conservative and the equivalence

\Map_{\mathcal{C}_{A}}(E\otimes_{k}A,-)\simeq\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(E,\mathrm{Forget}(-))\colon\mathcal{C}_{A}\to\Mod_{k}.

Lemma 2.1.19
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
smooth.
Let
\Spec(A)\in\dAff_{k}
and
F_{1},F_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{A}
with
F_{1}
compact and
F_{2}
pseudo-perfect.
Then
\Hom_{\mathcal{C}_{A}}(F_{1},F_{2})\in Perf(A)
.

It is enough to prove
Lemma

2.1.19
for a set of compact objects generating
\mathcal{C}_{A}^{\omega}
under finite colimits, shifts and retracts.
Let a compact generator
E\in\mathcal{C}
(
Proposition

2.1.12
).
By
Remark

2.1.18
, the object
E\otimes_{k}A
is a compact generator of
\mathcal{C}_{A}
.
By
Lemma

2.1.4
, we are reduced to prove that

\Hom_{\mathcal{C}_{A}}(E\otimes_{k}A,F_{2})\simeq F_{2}(E)\in\Perf(A),

which holds by our hypothesis that
F_{2}
is pseudo-perfect.
∎

Corollary 2.1.20
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
smooth.
Let
\Spec(A)\in\dAff_{k}
and
F_{1},F_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{A}
pseudo-perfect.
Then
\Hom_{\mathcal{C}_{A}}(F_{1},F_{2})\in Perf(A)
.

By
Lemma

2.1.17
we know that
F_{1}
is compact. The result then follows by
Lemma

2.1.19
.
∎

Lemma 2.1.21
.

Let
I\to\mathrm{Alg}(\Pr^{\mathrm{L}}_{k})
,
i\mapsto\mathcal{D}_{i}
, be a diagram and let
\mathcal{D}
be its limit.
Let
p_{i}\colon\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}_{i}
be the projection.
Let
A\in\mathrm{Alg}(\mathcal{D})
and put
A_{i}\coloneqq p_{i}(A)
.
Then an object
F\in\LMod_{A}(\mathcal{D})
is left-dualizable if and only if every projection
F_{i}\in\LMod_{A_{i}}(\mathcal{D}_{i})
is left-dualizable.

The direct implication is obvious since every
p_{i}\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}_{i}
is monoidal.
Let now
F\in\LMod_{A}(\mathcal{C})
and suppose that every projection
F_{i}\coloneqq p_{i}(F)
is left dualizable in
LMod_{A_{i}}(\mathcal{D}_{i})
.
Choose duality data
(F_{i},F_{i}^{\vee},\coev_{F_{i}},\ev_{F_{i}})
for each
i\in I
. By
[
Lur17
, Remark 4.6.2.11]
, these duality data are uniquely determined by the
F_{i}
’s up to a contractible space of choices.
Hence we have a compatible system of duality data. This defines a duality datum on
F
, proving that
F
is left-dualizable.
∎

Lemma 2.1.22
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
smooth and
X\in\dSt_{k}
.
Then every pseudo-perfect family
F\in\mathcal{C}_{X}
is left-dualizable.

By
Lemma

2.1.21
, it is enough to prove that for every
f\colon\Spec(B)\to X
, the object
f^{\ast}F\in\mathcal{C}_{B}
is left-dualizable.
Hence we may assume that
X=\Spec(B)
.
By
Lemma

2.1.13
, we can assume that
\mathcal{C}=\LMod_{A}
with
A\in\mathrm{Alg}_{k}
smooth.
By
Example

2.1.15
, we have an equivalence

\mathcal{C}_{B}\simeq\LMod_{A\otimes_{k}B}(\Mod_{B}).

Moreover, the above equivalence makes the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Fun}^{\mathrm{st}}_{k}((\mathcal{C}^{\omega})^{op};\Mod_{B})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\LMod_{A}(\Mod_{k})\otimes_{k}\Mod_{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\LMod_{A\otimes_{k}B}(\Mod_{k})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Forget}\otimes_{k}\Id_{B}}

commute.
Since left-dualizability can be checked after applying the right diagonal arrow by
[
Lur17
, Proposition 4.6.4.12-(6)]
, we are left to show that
F(E)\in\Perf(B)
.
Since
F
is pseudo-perfect, the conclusion follows.
∎

Lemma 2.1.23
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
smooth.
Let
f\colon Y\to X
in
\dSt_{k}
and let
F_{1},F_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{X}
with
F_{1}
pseudo-perfect.
There is a canonical equivalence

f^{\ast}\Hom_{\mathcal{C}_{X}}(F_{1},F_{2})\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{C}_{B}}(f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{1},f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{2}).

By
Lemma

2.1.22
, the object
F_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{X}
is left-dualizable.
Since
f^{\ast}_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\mathcal{C}_{X}\to\mathcal{C}_{Y}
is monoidal, it follows that
f^{\ast}F_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{Y}
is left-dualizable.
Hence we get

f^{\ast}\Hom_{\mathcal{C}_{X}}(F_{1},F_{2})\simeq f^{\ast}(F_{2}\otimes_{X}F_{1}^{\vee})\simeq f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{2}\otimes_{Y}(f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{1})^{\vee}\simeq\Hom_{\mathcal{C}_{Y}}(f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{1},f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{2}).

∎

Corollary 2.1.24
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
smooth.
Let
X\in\dSt_{k}
and
F_{1},F_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{X}
pseudo-perfect.
Then
\Hom_{\mathcal{C}_{X}}(F_{1},F_{2})\in\Perf(X)
.

Since
\Perf(-)
satisfies faithfully flat descent by
[
Lur18
, Proposition 2.8.4.2 & Corollary D.6.3.3]
, the result follows by combining
Lemma

2.1.23
with
Corollary

2.1.20
.
∎

2.2.
Moduli of objects and
t
-structures

In this paragraph we introduce the moduli of objects of an
\infty
-category of
finite type
.
This construction firstly appeared in
[
TV07
]
, but we will follow the presentation given in
[
Por25
, Section 1.5]
.

Recollection 2.2.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
.
Consider the presheaf

\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\dAff_{k}^{op}\to\mathrm{Spc}

\Spec A\to\mathcal{C}_{A}^{\simeq}.

Since
\mathcal{C}
is dualizable (
Lemma

2.1.10
) and
\Mod(-)
satisfies faithfully flat descent (
[
Lur17
, Corollary D.6.3.3]
), the presheaf
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathcal{C}}
defines a derived stack over
k
.
The stack
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathcal{C}}
is too big and has no chance to be representable.
The derived moduli of objects of
\mathcal{C}
is defined as the presheaf

\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}:\dAff_{k}^{op}\to\mathrm{Spc}

\Spec A\to\mathcal{C}_{A}^{pp}

where
\mathcal{C}_{A}^{pp}\subset\mathcal{C}_{A}
is the maximal groupoid spanned by pseudo-perfect families over
A
in the sense of
Definition

2.1.9
.
Since
\Perf(-)
satisfies hyper-descent (
[
Lur18
, Proposition 2.8.4.2-(10)]
), the sub-presheaf

\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}\subset\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathcal{C}}

is a derived substack by
Remark

2.1.2
.

The main theorem of
[
TV07
]
translates as:

Theorem 2.2.2
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
be of finite type.
Then
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}
is a locally geometric derived stack locally of finite presentation over
k
.
Furthermore, the tangent complex at a point
x\colon S\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}
corresponding to a pseudo-perfect family of objects
M_{x}\in\mathcal{C}_{\textsf{S}}
is given by

x^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}}\simeq\Hom(M_{x},M_{x})[1].

In this paper we are mainly interested in moduli of objects of abelian categories.
If
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
is equipped with a
t
-structure
\tau
, we can introduce a substack

\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}

whose classical truncation parametrizes families of objects in the heart
\tau
.
Under suitable assumption on
\tau
, the stack
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}
is an open substack of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}
, so that it is also locally geometric locally of finite type when
\mathcal{C}
is of finite type.

Definition 2.2.3
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with a
t
-structure
\tau
.
We say that

(1)

\tau
is
\omega
-accessible if the full subcategory
\mathcal{C}_{\leq 0}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
is stable under filtered colimits;

(2)

\tau
is left-complete if
\bigcap\mathcal{C}_{\geq n}\simeq 0
;

(3)

\tau
is right-complete if
\bigcap\mathcal{C}_{\leq n}\simeq 0
;

(4)

\tau
is non-degenerate if it is both right-complete and left-complete.

(5)

\tau
is admissible if it is
\omega
-accessible and non-degenerate.

Recollection 2.2.4
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
.
Then a morphism
f\colon\Spec(B)\to\Spec(A)
in
\dAff_{k}
corresponding to a map
A\to B
induces an adjunction

f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\colon\mathcal{C}_{A}\leftrightarrows\mathcal{C}_{B}\colon f_{\mathcal{C},\ast}

where
f_{\mathcal{C},\ast}
is the forgetful functor.
Denote by
p\colon\Spec(A)\to\Spec(k)
be the structural morphism.
As explained in
[
Por25
, Section I.5.4]
, given an
\omega
-accessible right-complete
t
-structure
\tau
on
\mathcal{C}
, we get an induced
\omega
-accessible right-complete
t
-structure
\tau_{A}
on
\mathcal{C}_{A}
for any
Spec(A)\in\dAff_{k}
by declaring that
F\in\mathcal{C}_{A}
is (co)connective if
p_{{}_{\mathcal{C}},\ast}(F)\in\mathcal{C}
is (co)connective.
Moreover
\tau_{A}
is non-degenerate if
\tau
is.

For
X\in\dSt_{k}
, we can also define an induced
t
-structure
\tau_{X}
on
\mathcal{C}_{X}
whose connective part is defined by descent.
That is,
F\in(\mathcal{C}_{X})_{\geq 0}
if and only if
f^{\ast}F\in(\mathcal{C}_{A})_{\geq 0}
for any
f\colon\Spec(A)\to X
.

Definition 2.2.5

(
[
Por25
, Definition I.5.28]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with an
\omega
-admissible right-complete
t
-structure
\tau
and
\Spec(A)\in\dAff_{k}
.
We say that a family
F\in\mathcal{C}_{A}
is
\tau
-flat over
\Spec(A)
(or
\tau_{A}
-flat) if for every
M\in\Mod_{A}^{\heartsuit}
one has
F\otimes_{A}M\in\mathcal{C}_{A}^{\heartsuit}
.

Remark 2.2.6
.

In the setting of
Definition

2.2.5
, when
A
is discrete, a
\tau
-flat family
F
over
A
lies in
\mathcal{C}_{A}^{\heartsuit}
.
Indeed in this case one has
A\in\Mod_{A}^{\heartsuit}
, so that
F\simeq F\otimes_{A}A\in\mathcal{C}_{A}^{\heartsuit}
.
The converse holds when
A
is a field.

We briefly recall why
\tau
-flatness behaves as in the geometric setting.

Lemma 2.2.7
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with an
\omega
-accessible right-complete
t
-structure
\tau
and let
f\colon\Spec(B)\to\Spec(A)
be flat.
Then the functor
f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\colon\mathcal{C}_{A}\to\mathcal{C}_{B}
is
t
-exact.

Let
F\in\mathcal{C}_{A}
.
By definition of induced
t
-structure (
2.2.4
), we have that
f^{\ast}F
is (co)connective if and only if
f_{\mathcal{C},\ast}f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F\simeq F\otimes_{B}A
is (co)connective.
By Lazard’s Theorem
[
Lur17
, Theorem 7.2.2.15]
, we have that
F\otimes_{A}B
is filtered colimit of direct sums of
F
.
Since the
t
-structure is
\omega
-accessible, it then follows that
F\otimes_{A}B
is (co)connective if
F
is.
∎

Lemma 2.2.8
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
.
Let
p\colon V\to X
be a flat effective epimorphism in
\dSt_{k}
and assume that
X
has affine diagonal and
V
is a derived scheme.
Then
f^{\ast}\colon\mathcal{C}_{X}\to\mathcal{C}_{V}
is
t
-exact and conservative.

Up to replacing
V
with a Zariski open cover, we can assume
V=\Spec(A)
.
Since
p
is a flat effective epimorphism, we have that
X
is equivalent to the Čech nerve
\Spec(A^{\bullet})
of
p
.
Hence we have an equivalence

\QCoh(X)\simeq\lim_{n\in\Delta_{s}}\Mod_{A^{n}}.

Since
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
is dualizable by
Lemma

2.1.10
, the functor

\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}-\colon\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}\to\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}

commutes with limits.
Hence we have an equivalence

\mathcal{C}_{X}\simeq\lim_{n\in\Delta_{s}}\mathcal{C}_{A^{n}}.

Under the above equivalence, the pullback
f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\colon\mathcal{C}_{X}\to\mathcal{C}_{A}
is identified with the projection
\lim_{n\in\Delta_{s}}\mathcal{C}_{A^{n}}\to\mathcal{C}_{A}
.
Moreover since
p
is a flat epimorphism and
X
has affine diagonal, all the transition functor are pullbacks along faithfully flat maps between derived affines.
Hence we are reduced to the affine case.
Since
\tau
is admissible, the statement follows by
Lemma

2.2.7
and
[
Lur18
, Proposition D.6.4.6]
.
∎

Corollary 2.2.9
.

In the setting of
Lemma

2.2.8
, a family
F\in\mathcal{C}_{X}
is (co)connective if and only if
f^{\ast}F\in\mathcal{C}_{V}
is (co)connective.

We prove the claim for connective families, the case of coconnective families being analogous.
We have
F\in(\mathcal{C}_{X})_{\geq 0}
if and only if
\tau_{\leq-1}F\simeq 0
in
\mathcal{C}_{X}
.
Since
f^{\ast}
is conservative and
t
-exact by
Lemma

2.2.8
, we have that
\tau_{\leq-1}F\simeq 0
in
\mathcal{C}_{X}
if and only if
\tau_{\leq-1}f^{\ast}F\simeq 0
in
\mathcal{C}_{V}
.
The latter condition is equivalent to
f^{\ast}F\in(\mathcal{C}_{V})_{\geq 0}
.
∎

Corollary 2.2.10
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
.
Consider a commutative square in
\dSt_{k}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

where
X,Y
have affine diagonal,
U,V
are derived schemes,
f,g
are flat and
p,q
are flat effective epimorphisms.
Then
f^{\ast}\colon\mathcal{C}_{X}\to\mathcal{C}_{Y}
is
t
-exact.

Let
F\in(\mathcal{C}_{Y})_{\geq 0}
.
By
Lemma

2.2.7
and
Corollary

2.2.9
, we have
g^{\ast}p^{\ast}F\in(\mathcal{C}_{U})_{\geq 0}
.
Since
g^{\ast}p^{\ast}F\simeq q^{\ast}f^{\ast}F
, applying again
Corollary

2.2.9
we get that
f^{\ast}F\in(\mathcal{C}_{X})_{\leq 0}
.
A similar proof shows that
f^{\ast}
preserve coconnective families.
∎

Lemma 2.2.11
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with an
\omega
-admissible right-complete
t
-structure
\tau
.
Let
f\colon\Spec(B)\to\Spec(A)
in
\dAff_{k}
.
If
F\in\mathcal{C}_{A}
is
\tau_{B}
-flat, then
f^{\ast}F
is
\tau_{B}
-flat.

By
[
Hai25
, Theorem 0.5]
, since in the affine case projection formula holds for the adjunction

f^{\ast}\colon\Mod_{A}\leftrightarrows\Mod_{B}\colon f_{\ast},

it also holds for the adjunction

f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\colon\mathcal{C}_{A}\leftrightarrows\mathcal{C}_{B}\colon f_{\mathcal{C},\ast}.

Since (co)connectivity can be tested after applying
\Id_{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{k}f_{\ast}
, the conclusion follows.
∎

Remark 2.2.12
.

Let
A\in\dAff_{k}
and
\mathcal{E}_{1},\mathcal{E}_{2}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{A}
.
The tensor product functor

-\otimes_{A}-\colon\mathcal{E}_{1}\times\mathcal{E}_{2}\to\mathcal{E}_{1}\otimes_{A}\mathcal{E}_{2}

preserves colimits separately in each variable.
Indeed since
A
is commutative, we have

\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{A}\coloneqq\Mod_{\Mod_{A}}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega})\simeq{}_{\Mod_{A}}\mathrm{BiMod}_{\Mod_{A}}(\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}).

By definition of
-\otimes_{A}-
via Bar construction (
[
Lur17
, Section 4.4.2]
), we have a factorization

\mathcal{E}_{1}\times\mathcal{E}_{2}\xrightarrow{-\otimes-}\mathcal{E}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{E}_{2}\xrightarrow{p}\mathcal{E}_{1}\otimes_{A}\mathcal{E}_{2}.

Since
p
is in
\Pr^{\mathrm{L}}
and the absolute tensor product
-\otimes-
commutes with colimits in each variable by
[
Lur17
, Remark 4.8.1.24]
, the claim follows.

Lemma 2.2.13
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
,
\Spec(A)\in\dAff_{k}
and
F\in\mathcal{C}_{A}
.
The functor

F\otimes_{A}-\colon\Mod_{A}\to\mathcal{C}_{A}

commutes with colimits.

It follows directly from
Remark

2.2.12
by taking
\mathcal{E}_{1}=\mathcal{C}_{A}
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}=\Mod_{A}
.
∎

Corollary 2.2.14
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with an
\omega
-accessible and right complete
t
-structure
\tau
.
Let
A\in\dAff_{k}
,
M\in(\Mod_{A})_{\geq 0}
and
F\in(\mathcal{C}_{A})_{\geq 0}
.
Then
F\otimes_{A}M\in(\mathcal{C}_{A})_{\geq 0}
.

Let

\mathcal{D}\coloneqq\left\{N\in\Mod_{A}\ \big|\ F\otimes_{A}N\in(\mathcal{C}_{A})_{\geq 0}\right\}\subset\Mod_{A}.

Since
(\mathcal{C}_{A})_{\geq 0}
is stable under colimits in
\mathcal{C}_{A}
, it follows from
Lemma

2.2.13
that the full subcategory
\mathcal{D}
is stable under colimits in
\Mod_{A}
.
Since
F
is connective, we have that
A\in\mathcal{D}
.
By
[
Lur17
, Proposition 7.1.1.13]
, we deduce that
(\Mod_{A})_{\geq 0}\subset\mathcal{D}
.
Hence
F\otimes_{A}M
is connective.
∎

Lemma 2.2.15
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
.
Let
f\colon\Spec(B)\to\Spec(A)
be a faithfully flat morphism.
Then
F\in\mathcal{C}_{A}
is
\tau_{A}
-flat if and only if
f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F
is
\tau_{B}
-flat.

The direct implication follows by
Lemma

2.2.11
.
Suppose now that
f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F
is
\tau_{B}
-flat and let
M\in\Mod_{A}^{\heartsuit}
.
By
Corollary

2.2.14
it is enough to prove that
\tau_{\geq 1}(F\otimes_{A}M)\simeq 0
.
Since
f
is faithfully flat and
\tau
is admissible, the functor
f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\colon\mathcal{C}_{A}\to\mathcal{C}_{B}
is conservative by
[
Lur18
, Proposition D.6.4.6]
.
Hence it is enough to prove that
f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}(\tau_{\geq 1}(F\otimes_{A}M))\simeq 0
.
Since
f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\colon\mathcal{C}_{A}\to\mathcal{C}_{B}
is
t
-exact by
Lemma

2.2.7
, we get

f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}(\tau_{\geq 1}(F\otimes M))\simeq\tau_{\geq 1}(f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}(F\otimes_{B}M))\simeq\tau_{\geq 1}(f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}(F)\otimes_{A}f^{\ast}(M)).

Since
f\colon\Spec(A)\to\Spec(B)
is flat, we have that
f^{\ast}(M)\in Mod_{A}^{\heartsuit}
.
Then by
\tau_{A}
-flatness of
f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}(F)
we get that
f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}(F)\otimes_{A}f^{\ast}(M)\in\mathcal{C}_{A}^{\heartsuit}
. Hence
\tau_{\geq 1}(f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}(F)\otimes_{A}f^{\ast}(M))\simeq 0
.
∎

Recollection 2.2.16
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
, and consider the sub-presheaf of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}

\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}:\dAff_{k}^{op}\to\mathrm{Spc}

sending
S\in\dAff_{k}
to the maximal
\infty
-groupoid of
\tau
-flat pseudo-perfect families of objects over
S
.
By
2.2.1
,
Lemma

2.2.11
and
Lemma

2.2.15
the presheaf
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}
is a derived substack of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}
.

Notation 2.2.17
.

We will denote by

\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}\colon\Aff_{k}^{op}\to\mathrm{Grpd}

the truncation of the derived stack
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}
.

Remark 2.2.18
.

The above notation is justified by the following: when testing on an underived affine, a
\tau
-flat family lies automatically in the heart (see
Remark

2.2.6
), while in general such family is only of Tor-amplitude
[0,0]
.

The following definition extends the notion of
\tau
-flat families for a general derived stack.

Definition 2.2.19
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
and let
X\in\dSt_{k}
.
A family
F\in\mathcal{C}_{X}
is
\tau_{X}
-flat if for every
\Spec(B)\to X
, the pullback
f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F\in\mathcal{C}_{B}
is
\tau_{B}
-flat.

Lemma 2.2.20
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
.
Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a morphism in
\dSt_{k}
.
If
F\in\mathcal{C}_{Y}
is
\tau_{Y}
-flat, then
f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F
is
\tau_{X}
-flat.

We need to prove that for every
g:\Spec(B)\to X
,
g_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}(f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F)
is
\tau_{B}
-flat.
Since we have an equivalence
g_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}(f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F)\simeq(f\circ g)_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F
, this holds since
F
is
\tau_{Y}
-flat.
∎

Lemma 2.2.21
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
.
Let
f\colon Y\to X
be a flat effective epimorphism, with
Y
quasi-geometric in the sense of
[
Lur18
, Definition 9.1.0.1]
.
Then
F\in\mathcal{C}_{X}
is
\tau_{X}
-flat if and only if
f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F
is
\tau_{Y}
-flat.

Let us notice that by definition of quasi-geometric stack there exists a flat epimorphism
p\colon\Spec(A)\to Y
.
If we can prove the claim when the source is derived affine, then we deduce the general claim by applying it to
f\circ p\colon\Spec(A)\to X
and to
p\colon\Spec(A)\to Y
.
Hence we can assume
Y=\Spec(A)
.
The direct implication follows from
Lemma

2.2.20
.
For the converse, suppose that
f^{\ast}F\in\mathcal{C}_{A}
is
\tau_{A}
-flat.
We need to show that for every
g\colon\Spec(B)\to X
we have that
g_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F
is
\tau_{B}
-flat.
Since
f
is an effective epimorphism, there exists an fppf cover
\pi\colon\Spec(B^{\prime})\to\Spec(B)
such that there exists a lift
\widetilde{g}:\Spec(B^{\prime})\to X
of
g
as in the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spec(A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spec(B^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spec(B)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

By
Lemma

2.2.15
,
g_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F
is
\tau_{B}
-flat if and only if
\pi_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}g_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F\simeq\widetilde{g}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F
is
\tau_{B^{\prime}}
-flat.
The result then follows from
Lemma

2.2.11
.
∎

The key property for a
t
-structure to define an open substack of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}
is linked to the following definition:

Definition 2.2.22
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\Pr_{k}^{L,\omega}
equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
.
Let
\textsf{S}\in\dAff_{k}
and let
F\in\mathcal{C}_{S}
.
The flat locus of
F
is the functor

\Phi_{F}\colon(\dAff_{k})^{op}_{/_{S}}\to\Spc

\displaystyle(T\xrightarrow{f}\textsf{S})\mapsto\left\{\begin{aligned} &\ast\ \ \text{if $f^{\ast}(F)$ is $\tau$-flat relative to $T$,}\\
&\emptyset\ \ \ \ \text{otherwise.}\end{aligned}\right.

Definition 2.2.23
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\Pr_{k}^{L,\omega}
equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
.
We say that
\tau
universally satisfies openness of flatness if for every
S\in\dAff_{k}
and every
F\in\mathcal{C}_{S}
the flat locus
\Phi_{F}
of
F
is an open derived subscheme of
S
.

Proposition 2.2.24

(
[
Por25
, Proposition I.5.35]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\Pr_{k}^{L,\omega}
be of finite type equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
.
If
\tau
universally satisfies openness of flatness, then the structural morphism

\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}

is representable by a Zariski open immersion.
In particular,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}
is a locally geometric derived stack, locally of finite presentation.

A priori,
Proposition

2.2.24
only guarantees that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}
and its truncation
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
have an open exhaustion by (derived) algebraic stacks.
When
\Spec(A)\in\dAff_{k}
is discrete, the
\infty
-grupoid
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}(\Spec(A))
takes values in
1
-grupoids by
Remark

2.2.6
.
Hence
\mathcal{M}_{C}^{\heartsuit}
is
1
-truncated in the sense of
[
TV08
]
.
Then
[
TV07
, Lemma 2.19]
implies the following

Corollary 2.2.25
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\Pr_{k}^{L,\omega}
be of finite type equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
.
If
\tau
universally satisfies openness of flatness, then
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}
is a
1
-Artin stack.
In particular its truncation
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
is an algebraic stack locally of finite presentation.

Example 2.2.26
.

For an example satisfying universal openness of flatness let us refer to
Theorem

4.2.3
.

2.3.
An Hartogs-like lemma for flatness

In this paragraph we reproduce a fundamental result of
[
AHLH23
]
in our setting, namely
[
AHLH23
, Lemma 7.15]
.
The proof will essentially be the same.
This lemma is crucial in the construction of good moduli spaces for moduli of objects.

Lemma 2.3.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with an
\omega
-accessible right-complete
t
-structure
\tau
.
Let
\Spec(A)\in\dAff_{k}
and
F\in\mathcal{C}_{A}
a
\tau_{A}
-flat family.
Then the functor

F\otimes_{A}-\colon\Mod_{A}\to\mathcal{C}_{A}

is exact.
In particular it restricts to an exact functor

F\otimes_{A}-\colon\Mod_{A}^{\heartsuit}\to\mathcal{C}_{A}^{\heartsuit}.

Thus the functor
F\otimes_{A}-
is commutes with (finite) colimits by
Lemma

2.2.13
.
Hence the first claim follows by
[
Lur17
, Proposition 1.1.4.1]
.
The second one follows from the first as exact sequences in the heart of a
t
-structure are (by definition) fiber/cofiber sequences where all the terms are in the heart.
∎

Remark 2.3.2
.

Assume that
\Spec(A)
is discrete.
Differently to our convention, flat families over
\Spec(A)
are defined in
[
AHLH23
]
as families
F\in\mathcal{C}_{A}^{\heartsuit}
such that

\pi_{0}(F\otimes_{A}-)\colon\Mod_{A}^{\heartsuit}\to\mathcal{C}_{A}^{\heartsuit}

is an exact functor.

Lemma

2.3.1
shows that
\tau
-flat families in the sense of
Definition

2.2.5
are flat in the sense of
[
AHLH23
]
.
The two notions agree when the
t
-structure is left-complete.

Proposition 2.3.3

(
[
HL14
, Lemma 6.2.2]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
.
Let
\Spec(A)\in\dAff_{k}
discrete.
For a family
F\in\mathcal{C}_{A}
over
A
the following are equivalent:

(1)

F
is
\tau_{A}
-flat;

(2)

F\in\mathcal{C}_{A}^{\heartsuit}
and
\pi_{0}(F\otimes_{A}-)\colon\Mod_{A}^{\heartsuit}\to\mathcal{C}_{A}^{\heartsuit}
is an exact functor.

That (1) implies (2) follows directly from
Remark

2.2.6
and
Lemma

2.3.1
.
Suppose now that

\pi_{0}(F\otimes_{A}-)\colon\Mod_{A}^{\heartsuit}\to\mathcal{C}_{A}^{\heartsuit}

is exact and consider
M\in\Mod_{A}^{\heartsuit}
.
We need to show that
F\otimes_{A}M\in\mathcal{C}_{A}^{\heartsuit}
.
By
Corollary

2.2.14
, it is enough to show that
\tau_{\geq 1}(F\otimes_{A}M)\simeq 0
.
A choice of generators of
M
gives a presentation

(2.3.4)

0\to K\to A^{S}\to M\to 0.

By
Lemma

2.3.1
, applying
F\otimes_{A}-
to (
2.3.4
) yields a fiber/cofiber sequence

F\otimes_{A}K\to F\otimes_{A}A^{S}\to F\otimes_{A}M.

The associated long exact sequence of homotopy groups yields an exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}(F\otimes_{A}A^{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{1}(F\otimes_{A}M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{0}(F\otimes_{A}K)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{0}(F\otimes_{A}A^{S})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{0}(F\otimes_{A}M)}}

By hypothesis, the map

\pi_{1}(F\otimes_{A}A^{S})\to\pi_{1}(F\otimes_{A}M)

is an epimorphism.
Moreover since
F\in\mathcal{C}_{A}^{\heartsuit}
and
\mathcal{C}_{A}^{\heartsuit}
is closed under arbitrary direct sums, we have

F\otimes_{A}A^{S}\simeq\bigoplus_{S}F\in\mathcal{C}_{A}^{\heartsuit}.

Thus we get
\pi_{i}(F\otimes_{A}A^{S})=0
for any
i>0
.
Hence we get
\pi_{1}(F\otimes_{A}M)=0
and

\pi_{i+1}(F\otimes_{A}M)\xlongrightarrow{\sim}\pi_{i}(F\otimes_{A}K)

for any
i>0
.
This two facts allow to conclude by induction that
\pi_{i}(F\otimes_{A}M)=0
for any
i>0
.
Indeed, we can apply recursively the same argument where we replace
M
by
K
until we get
i=1
.
Since
\tau_{A}
is left-complete, we get

\tau_{\geq 1}(F\otimes_{A}M)\in\bigcap_{n\geq 1}(\mathcal{C}_{A})_{\geq n}\simeq 0.

∎

The following Lemmas are analogues of
[
AZ01
, Lemma C1.12 & Proposition C1.13]
to our setup.

Lemma 2.3.5

(local criterion for flatness)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with an
\omega
-accessible right-complete
t
-structure
\tau
and let
R
be a discrete noetherian commutative ring.
For
F\in\mathcal{C}_{R}
, the following statements are equivalent:

(1)

F\in\mathcal{C}_{R}
is
\tau_{R}
-flat;

(2)

for every prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}\subset R
we have
F\otimes_{R}R/\mathfrak{p}\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
;

That (1) implies (2) follows by definition of
\tau
-flatness.
Assume now that (2) holds.
Let
M\in\Mod_{R}^{\heartsuit}
.
We need to prove that
F\otimes_{R}M\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
.
Write
M
as a filtered colimit of its finitely generated submodule.
Since
\tau
is
\omega
-accessible and tensor product commutes with filtered colimits by
Lemma

2.2.13
, we can assume that
M
is finitely generated.
By
[
Sta25
,
Lemma 00L0
]
, there exists a filtration

0=M_{0}\subset M_{1}\subset\cdots\subset M_{n}=M

such that
M_{i}/M_{i-1}\simeq R/\mathfrak{p}_{i}
for some prime ideal
\mathfrak{p}_{i}\subset R
.
We prove that
F\otimes M_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
by induction on
0\leq i\leq n
.
For
i=0
is obvious.
Let now
0\leq i<n
and assume that
F\otimes_{R}M_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
.
By
Lemma

2.3.1
, we have a fiber/cofiber sequence

F\otimes_{R}M_{i}\to F\otimes_{R}M_{i+1}\to F\otimes_{R}R/\mathfrak{p}_{i+1}.

The first and third term belong to
\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
by hypothesis.
Since
\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
is closed under extensions, we get that
F\otimes_{R}M_{i+1}\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
.
∎

Corollary 2.3.6
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with an
\omega
-accessible right-complete
t
-structure
\tau
and let
R
be a DVR over
k
with uniformizer
\pi
.
Then
F\in\mathcal{C}_{R}
is
\tau_{R}
-flat if and only if
F\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
and the multiplication by
F\xrightarrow{\pi}F
is injective.

The only prime ideals are
(0),(\pi)\subset R
.
We have that
F\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
if and only if
F\otimes_{R}R/(0)\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
.
Let
\kappa\coloneqq R/(\pi)
.
By
Lemma

2.3.5
, it is enough to prove that for
F\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
we have
F\otimes_{R}\kappa\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
if and only if
F\xrightarrow{\pi}F
is injective.
By
Lemma

2.3.1
, the short exact sequence

0\to R\xrightarrow{\pi}R\to\kappa\to 0

induces a fiber cofiber sequence

F\xrightarrow{\pi}F\to F\otimes_{R}\kappa.

Taking the associated long exact sequence of homotopy groups yields an exact sequence

0\to\pi_{1}(F\otimes_{s}\kappa)\to F\xrightarrow{\pi}F\to\pi_{0}(F\otimes_{R}\kappa)\to 0

and
\pi_{i}(F\otimes_{R}\kappa)\simeq 0
for every
i\neq 0,1
.
Hence we get that
F\otimes_{R}\kappa\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
if and only if
F\xrightarrow{\pi}F
is injective.
∎

In the next paragraph we give a more explicit description of pseudo-perfect
\tau
-flat families in a case we are particularly interested into.

Recollection 2.3.7
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
.
Let
X=\Spec(R)\in\dAff_{k}
be discrete local regular noetherian of dimension 2.
Let
j\colon U\to X
be the complement of the unique closed point and let
x,y\in R
be a regular sequence.
By
[
Lur11b
, Lemma 2.11]
, a family
F\in\mathcal{C}_{U}
corresponds to the following data:

(i)

Families

F_{x}\in\mathcal{C}_{R_{x}},\ \ F_{y}\in\mathcal{C}_{R_{x}},\ \ F_{xy}\in\mathcal{C}_{R_{xy}};

(ii)

descent data

F_{y}\otimes_{R_{y}}R_{xy}\simeq F_{xy}\simeq F_{x}\otimes_{R_{x}}R_{xy}.

By
Lemma

2.2.21
, a
\tau
-flat family on
U
is given by (i) and (ii) as above, with the requirement that
F_{x}
,
F_{y}
and
F_{xy}
are
\tau
-flat.

Recollection 2.3.8
.

Let
A\to\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
,
\alpha\mapsto\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}
, be a diagram in
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
, and let
\mathcal{D}
be its limit.
Let
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
in
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
and put
f_{\alpha}\coloneqq p_{\alpha}\circ f
for every
\alpha\in A
, where
p_{\alpha}\colon\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}
is the projection.
For
\alpha\in A
, let
g_{\alpha}\colon D_{\alpha}\to\mathcal{C}
be the right adjoint to
f_{\alpha}
.
One immediately checks that the right adjoint
g\colon\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{C}
to
f\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
is computed by

g\colon\mathcal{D}\to\mathcal{C}

d\mapsto\lim_{\alpha}g_{\alpha}\circ p_{\alpha}(d)

Lemma 2.3.9
.

In the setting of
2.3.7
, let
F\in\mathcal{C}_{U}
be a family over
U
.
We have

j_{\ast}F\simeq\fib(F_{x}\oplus F_{y}\to F_{xy})

where
F_{x}
,
F_{y}
and
F_{xy}
are considered as families in
\mathcal{C}_{R}
via the respective forgetful functors.

Since
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
is dualizable by
Lemma

2.1.10
, the functor

\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}-\colon\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}\to\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}

commutes with limits. Thus
\mathcal{C}_{U}
fits in a pullback square in
\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}_{U}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}_{R_{y}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}_{R_{x}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{C}_{R_{xy}}\ .}}

By
2.3.8
, the family
j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F
fits in a pullback square in
\mathcal{C}_{R}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{j_{\ast}F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{y}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{x}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{xy}\ .}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

Since
\mathcal{C}_{R}
is stable, we can rewrite the above as

j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F\simeq\fib(F_{x}\oplus F_{y}\to F_{xy}).

∎

Corollary 2.3.10
.

In the setting of
2.3.7
, suppose that
F\in\mathcal{C}_{U}
is
\tau
-flat over
U
. Then

\pi_{0}j_{\ast}F\simeq\mathrm{Ker}(F_{x}\oplus F_{y}\to F_{xy})

where
F_{x}
,
F_{y}
and
F_{xy}
are considered as families in
\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
via the respective forgetful functors.

By
\tau_{U}
-flatness of
F
and exactness of forgetful functors, we have that
F_{x},F_{y},F_{xy}\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
.
The statement then follows by
Lemma

2.3.9
.
∎

Remark 2.3.11
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
and let
f\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
in
\dSt_{k}
.
By definition of
\tau_{\mathcal{X}}
and
\tau_{\mathcal{Y}}
, the functor
f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\colon\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{Y}}\to\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}}
preserves connective objects.
In particular, the adjunction
f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\dashv(f_{\mathcal{C}})_{\ast}
restricts to an adjunction

\pi_{0}f_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\colon\mathcal{C}_{Y}^{\heartsuit}\leftrightarrows\mathcal{C}_{X}^{\heartsuit}\colon\pi_{0}(f_{\mathcal{C}})_{\ast}

Lemma 2.3.12
.

In the setting of
2.3.7
, the functor
j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}\colon C_{U}\to\mathcal{C}_{X}
is fully faithful.

We need to prove that the counit
j_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}\to\Id_{\mathcal{C}_{U}}
is an equivalence.
Given
F\in\mathcal{C}_{U}
, let
F_{x},F_{y},F_{xy}
as in
2.3.7
.
By
Lemma

2.3.9
, we have
j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F\simeq\fib(F_{x}\oplus F_{y}\to F_{xy})
.
Thus restricting to
R_{x}
(respectively,
R_{y}
,
R_{xy}
), we get back
F_{x}
(respectively,
F_{y}
,
F_{xy}
).
Hence
j_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F\simeq F
.
∎

Corollary 2.3.13
.

In the setting of
2.3.7
, the functor
\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}\colon C_{U}^{\heartsuit}\to\mathcal{C}_{X}^{\heartsuit}
is fully faithful.

We need to prove that the counit
\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}\to\Id_{\mathcal{C}_{U}^{\heartsuit}}
is an equivalence.
Since
j_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}
is
t
-exact by
Corollary

2.2.10
, we have

\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}\simeq j_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}\simeq\pi_{0}(j_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}).

The conclusion follows by
Lemma

2.3.12
.
∎

Lemma 2.3.14
.

In the setting of
2.3.7
, the functor
\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}\colon C_{U}^{\heartsuit}\to\mathcal{C}_{X}^{\heartsuit}
preserve
\tau
-flat objects.

Let
F\in\mathcal{C}_{U}^{\heartsuit}
be
\tau
-flat over
U
.
By
Lemma

2.3.5
, we need to prove that for any
\mathfrak{q}\in\Spec(R)
, we have
\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F\otimes_{k}R/\mathfrak{q}\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
.
Since
j_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}
is
t
-exact by
Corollary

2.2.10
, we have
j_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F\simeq\pi_{0}F\simeq F
.
Hence the statement is clear for
\mathfrak{q}\in U
and we are reduced to prove that
\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F\otimes_{k}\kappa\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
, where
\kappa\coloneqq R/(x,y)
.
Consider the free resolution

0\to R\to R\oplus R\to R\to\kappa\to 0.

It is enough to show that tensoring this with
j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F
yields an exact sequence

(2.3.15)

0\to\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F\to\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F\oplus\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F\to\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F.

Consider the exact sequence

0\to\mathcal{O}_{U}\to\mathcal{O}_{U}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{U}\to\mathcal{O}_{U}\to 0.

Tensoring with
F
yields an exact sequence over
U
by
Proposition

2.3.3
.
Applying
\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}
one gets the sequence in (
2.3.15
), which is exact since
\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}
is exact on the left (because
j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}
preserve coconnective objects).
∎

We are now ready to prove that
[
AHLH23
, Lemma 7.15]
carries on in our context with the same proof.

Lemma 2.3.16
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
.
Let
X
be a regular noetherian scheme of dimension 2 over
k
.
Let
j\colon U\to X
be complement of a dimension
0
closed subscheme of
X
.
Then the adjunction

\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\colon\mathcal{C}_{X}^{\heartsuit}\leftrightarrows\mathcal{C}_{U}^{\heartsuit}\colon\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}

induces an equivalence between the full subcategories of
\tau
-flat families over
U
and
\tau
-flat families over
X
.

By descent we reduce to the local situation of
2.3.7
.
By
Corollary

2.3.13
and
Lemma

2.3.14
, it is enough to prove that for
F\in\mathcal{C}_{R}
a
\tau
-flat family over
X=\Spec(R)
, the unit morphism
F\to\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}\pi_{0}j^{\ast}F
is an equivalence.
Localizing
F
at
x,y
and
xy
, we get a
\tau
-flat family
j^{\ast}F
over
U
.
By
Lemma

2.3.1
, tensoring the exact sequence in
Mod_{R}^{\heartsuit}

0\to R\to R_{x}\oplus R_{y}\to R_{xy}

with
F
yields an exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}

0\to F\to F_{x}\oplus F_{y}\to F_{xy}.

Hence
F\simeq\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}j_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F\simeq\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F
, where the second equivalence follows by
t
-exactness of
j_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\colon\mathcal{C}_{X}\to\mathcal{C}_{U}
(
Corollary

2.2.10
).
∎

Corollary 2.3.17
.

In the setting of
2.3.16
, let
G
be a smooth affine algebraic group over
k
equipped with an action on
X
which restricts to an action on
U
.
Set
\mathcal{X}\coloneqq[X/G]
,
\mathfrak{U}\coloneqq[U/G]
and
\overline{j}\colon\mathfrak{U}\to\mathcal{X}
the open immersion.
Then the adjunction

\pi_{0}\overline{j}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\colon\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\heartsuit}\leftrightarrows\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{U}}^{\heartsuit}\colon\pi_{0}\overline{j}_{\mathcal{C},\ast}

induces an equivalence between the full subcategories
\tau
-flat families over
\mathfrak{U}
and
\tau
-flat families over
\mathcal{X}
.

Let
p\colon U\to\mathfrak{U}
and
q\colon X\to X
be the atlas map. Then we have a pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{U}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{j}}

By
[
Hai25
, Theorem 0.5]
(see also
Remark

2.1.2
), flat base change for quasi-coherent sheaves implies flat base change in our setting by applying
\Id_{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{k}-
, that is, we have an equivalence of functors

q_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\overline{j}_{\mathcal{C},\ast}\simeq j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}p_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\colon\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{U}}\to\mathcal{C}_{X}.

By
Lemma

2.2.8
and
Corollary

2.2.10
, the functors
\overline{j}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast},j_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast},p_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast},q_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}
are
t
-exact and
p_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast},q_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}
are conservative.
It then follows formally from
Lemma

2.3.16
that the unit and counit of the adjunction

\pi_{0}\overline{j}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\colon\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{X}}^{\heartsuit}\leftrightarrows\mathcal{C}_{\mathfrak{U}}^{\heartsuit}\colon\pi_{0}\overline{j}_{\mathcal{C},\ast}

are equivalent to the identity functors.
We are left to show that
\pi_{0}\overline{j}_{\mathcal{C}}
sends
\tau
-flat families over
\mathfrak{U}
to
\tau
-flat families over
\mathcal{X}
.
By
Lemma

2.2.20
, the family
p^{\ast}F
is
\tau_{U}
-flat.
Hence it follows by
Lemma

2.3.16
that
\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}p^{\ast}F\simeq\pi_{0}q_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\overline{j}_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F
is
\tau_{X}
-flat.
Since
q^{\ast}
is exact, we have that
\pi_{0}q_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\overline{j}_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F\simeq q_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}(\pi_{0}\overline{j}_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F)
.
The result then follows by applying
Lemma

2.2.11
.
∎

2.4.
Existence criteria for good moduli spaces

In this paragraph we recall the definition of good moduli spaces and an existence criterion for them.
References are given to
[
Alp13
,
AHLH23
]
.

Definition 2.4.1

(
[
Alp13
, Definition 4.1]
)
.

Let
q\colon\mathcal{X}\to X
be a qcqs morphism over an algebraic space
S
with
\mathcal{X}
an algebraic stack and
X
an algebraic space.
We say that
q\colon\mathcal{X}\to X
is a good moduli space if the following properties are satisfied:

(1)

The functor
q_{\ast}\colon\QCoh(\mathcal{X})\to\QCoh(X)
is
t
-exact;

(2)

we have
q_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{X}
.

For completeness, we recall some of the main properties of good moduli spaces:

Theorem 2.4.2

(
[
Alp13
, Proposition 4.5 & Theorem 4.16 & Theorem 6.6]
)
.

Let
q\colon\mathcal{X}\to X
be a good moduli space.
Then

(1)

The functor
q^{\ast}\colon\QCoh(X)^{\heartsuit}\to\QCoh(\mathcal{X})^{\heartsuit}
is fully faithful;

(2)

the map
q
is universally closed and surjective;

(3)

if
Z_{1},Z_{2}
are closed substacks of
\mathcal{X}
, then

\mathrm{Im}(Z_{1})\cap\mathrm{Im}(Z_{2})=\mathrm{Im}(Z_{1}\cap Z_{2})

where the intersections and images are scheme-theoretic.

(4)

for
\kappa
an algebraically closed field over
k
,
\kappa
-points of
X
are
\kappa
-points of
\mathcal{X}
up to closure equivalence (see
[
Alp13
, Theorem 4.16-(iv)]
for a precise statement);

(5)

If
\mathcal{X}
is reduced (resp., quasi-
compact, connected, irreducible), then
X
is also.
If
\mathcal{X}
is locally noetherian
and normal, then
X
is also.

(6)

if
\mathcal{X}
is locally noetherian, then
X
is locally noetherian and
q_{\ast}\colon\QCoh(\mathcal{X})^{\heartsuit}\to\QCoh(X)^{\heartsuit}
preserves coherence;

(7)

if
k
is excellent and
\mathcal{X}
is of finite type, then
X
is of finite type;

(8)

if
\mathcal{X}
is locally noetherian, then
q\colon\mathcal{X}\to X
is universal for maps to algebraic spaces.

The necessary and sufficient conditions of
[
AHLH23
]
for the existence of a good moduli spaces are given in terms of valuative criteria for two stacks that we now introduce.
In what follows we fix
R
a DVR over
k
.
Let
\pi\in R
be a uniformizer and
\kappa
the residue field of
R
.
The first stack we have to consider is

\Theta_{R}\coloneqq[\Spec(R[x])/\mathbb{G}_{m,R}],

which has an unique closed point, denoted by
0
.
Explicitly,

0\coloneqq[\Spec(\kappa)/\mathbb{G}_{m,R}].

Definition 2.4.3
.

We say that
\mathcal{X}\in\mathrm{St}_{k}
is
\Theta
-reductive if for any DVR
R
and any diagram of solid arrows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Theta_{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Theta_{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!\ \widetilde{f}}

there exists a unique extension
\widetilde{f}
of
f
making the above triangle commutes.

The second stack we have to consider is

\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\coloneqq\left[{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\scriptstyle{\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.6994pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}$}}}\right],

which has a unique closed point, denoted by
0
.
Explicitly,

0\coloneqq[\Spec(\kappa)/\mathbb{G}_{m,R}].

Definition 2.4.4
.

We say that
\mathcal{X}\in\mathrm{St}_{k}
is
\mathrm{S}
-complete if for any DVR
R
and any diagram of solid arrows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!\ \widetilde{f}}

there exists a unique extension
\widetilde{f}
of
f
making the above triangle commute.

Let us now state the main theorem of
[
AHLH23
]
:

Theorem 2.4.5

(
[
AHLH23
, Theorem A]
)
.

Let
S
be an algebraic space of characteristic
0
.
Let
\mathcal{X}
be an algebraic stack of finite presentation with affine stabilizers and separated diagonal over
S
.
Then
\mathcal{X}
admits a separated good moduli space
X
if and only if
\mathcal{X}
is
\Theta
-reductive and
\mathrm{S}
-complete.

Moduli of
\tau
-flat families have affine diagonal, as shown by the following:

Lemma 2.4.6

(
[
AHLH23
, Lemma 7.20]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
be of finite type equipped with be an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
universally satisfying openness of flatness.
Then
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
has affine diagonal.

By
Corollary

2.2.25
we have that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
is an algebraic stack locally of finite presentation.
Then the proof of
[
AHLH23
, Lemma 7.20]
carries on in our setup.
∎

Remark 2.4.7
.

In our setup having affine diagonal (thus affine stabilizers) and being locally of finite presentation come for free (see
Lemma

2.4.6
and
Corollary

2.2.25
), so that in
Theorem

2.4.5
one only needs to check quasi-compactness,
\Theta
-reductiveness and
\mathrm{S}
-completeness.
The stack
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}
will essentially never be quasi-compact, but
\Theta
-reductiveness and
\mathrm{S}
-completeness are stable under taking closed substacks.

Finally, let us recall that a notion of derived good moduli spaces has been introduced in
[
AHPS23
]
.
The following is the main result of that paper:

Theorem 2.4.8

(
[
AHPS23
, Theorem 2.12]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a geometric derived stack.

(1)

If
t_{0}\mathcal{X}
admits a good moduli space
t_{0}q\colon t_{0}\mathcal{X}\to t_{0}X
, then
\mathcal{X}
admits a derived good moduli space
q^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{X}\to X
such that
t_{0}q^{\prime}\simeq t_{0}q
.

(2)

If
\mathcal{X}
admits a derived good moduli space
q\colon\mathcal{X}\to X
, then
t_{0}q
is a good moduli space for
t_{0}X
.

3.
Good moduli spaces for
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}

In this section we prove the existence of good moduli spaces for
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
. In particular, we show that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
is
\Theta
-reductive and
\mathrm{S}
-complete under the following

Assumption 3.0.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in Pr^{L,\omega}_{k}
smooth equipped with an admissible
t
-structure.
For any DVR
R
over
k
, the abelian category
\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
has the following property: any subobject (equivalently, quotient) of a pseudo-perfect object is also pseudo-perfect.

Remark 3.0.2
.

For what follows, it is enough to ask that
3.0.1
holds for any subobject of a
\tau_{R}
-flat object.
Moreover in
[
AHLH23
]
it is shown that it is enough to ask that
3.0.1
holds for any DVR that is essentially of finite type.

Let us mention some cases in which the Assumption is satisfied.

Example 3.0.3
.

Standard
t
-structures on functor categories (e.g., quiver representations).
This is nothing but the fact that a submodule of a finitely generated
R
-module is still finitely generated when
R
is noetherian.

Example 3.0.4
.

Perverse sheaves on nice enough stratified spaces.
See
Proposition

4.1.8
.

Example 3.0.5
.

If
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
is smooth and proper,
\mathcal{C}^{\heartsuit}
is locally noetherian and
R
is essentially of finite type. This follows by
Lemma

2.1.17
and the work of Artin-Zhang
[
AZ01
]
.

Example 3.0.6
.

Quasi-coherent sheaves on a quasi-compact separated algebraic space locally of finite presentation. This follows by
[
BZNP17
, Theorem 3.0.2]
.

3.1.
\Theta
-reductiveness

We will prove in this paragraph that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
is
\Theta
-complete.
In order to proceed with this verification, we will characterize explicitly
\tau_{\Theta_{R}}
-flat pseudo-perfect families in
\mathcal{C}_{\Theta_{R}}
in
Proposition

3.1.11
.

Notation 3.1.1
.

For
\mathcal{E}
a stable
\infty
-category, put

\Rep(\mathbb{Z};\mathcal{E})\coloneqq\Fun(\mathbb{Z}^{op},\mathcal{E})\ .

Recall from
Theorem

A.1.1
that

\QCoh(\Theta_{R})\simeq\Rep(\mathbb{Z},\Mod_{R}).

By
[
BZFN10
, Corollary 3.22]
we have

\Perf(\Theta_{R})=\QCoh(\Theta_{R})^{\omega}\simeq\Rep(\mathbb{Z},\Mod_{R})^{\omega}\ .

Consider the inclusion
i_{n}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{\leq n}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Z}
.
The pullback
i_{n}^{\ast}
along
i_{n}
has a left adjoint
i_{n,!}
given by left Kan extension.
Put

(-)_{\leq n}\coloneqq i_{n,!}i_{n}^{\ast}\colon\Rep(\mathbb{Z};\mathcal{E})\to\Rep(\mathbb{Z};\mathcal{E})\ .

Explicitly, on an object

E_{\bullet}\colon\ \cdots\xrightarrow{f_{n+1}}E_{n}\xrightarrow{f_{n}}E_{n-1}\xrightarrow{f_{n-1}}\cdots

we have

(E_{\bullet})_{\leq n}\colon\ \cdots\rightarrow 0\rightarrow E_{n}\xrightarrow{f_{n}}E_{n-1}\xrightarrow{f_{n-1}}\cdots

Lemma 3.1.2
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a stable
\infty
-category.
Suppose we are given an object

E_{\bullet}\colon\ \cdots\xrightarrow{f_{n+1}}E_{n}\xrightarrow{f_{n}}E_{n-1}\xrightarrow{f_{n-1}}\cdots

in
\Rep(\mathbb{Z};\mathcal{E})
.
Then the canonical comparison

(3.1.3)

\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(E_{\bullet})_{\leq n}\to E_{\bullet}

is an equivalence.

It is enough to show that
(
3.1.3
)
is an equivalence after evaluating on each
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. But for
n>m
we have that
(E_{\bullet})_{\leq n}(m)=E_{m}
, so that in the colimit the evaluation at
m
stabilizes to
E_{m}
with transition maps given by
\Id_{E_{m}}
.
∎

Corollary 3.1.4
.

In the setting of
Lemma

3.1.2
, if
E_{\bullet}
is compact we have
E_{n}\simeq 0
for
n\gg 0
.

Since
E_{\bullet}
is compact, it follows from
Lemma

3.1.2
that

\Id_{E_{\bullet}}\in\Map(E_{\bullet},E_{\bullet})\simeq\Map(E_{\bullet},\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(E_{\bullet})_{\leq n})\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Map(E_{\bullet},(E_{\bullet})_{\leq n}).

Thus
\Id_{E_{\bullet}}
factors through
(E_{\bullet})_{\leq k}
for some
k\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Hence
E_{n}\simeq 0
for every
k>n
.
∎

Recollection 3.1.5
.

Let us choose a coordinate
x
on
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R}
.
Recall the equivalence

\QCoh(\Theta_{R})\simeq\Mod_{R[x]}(\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds}))\simeq\Rep(\mathbb{Z},\Mod_{R}).

from
Theorem

A.1.1
.
It follows that we have equivalences

(3.1.6)

\mathcal{C}_{\Theta_{R}}=\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}\QCoh(\Theta_{R})\simeq\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}\Rep(\mathbb{Z},\Mod_{R})\simeq\Rep(\mathbb{Z},\mathcal{C}_{R})\ .

Hence a family
F_{\bullet}\in\mathcal{C}_{\Theta_{R}}
can be represented by

F_{\bullet}\colon\ \cdots\xrightarrow{x}F_{n}\xrightarrow{x}F_{n-1}\xrightarrow{x}\cdots.

where
F_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{R}
for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Corollary 3.1.7
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
smooth and
\Spec(R)\in\dAff_{k}
discrete.
Let
F_{\bullet}\in\mathcal{C}_{\Theta_{R}}
and represent it

F_{\bullet}\colon\ \cdots\xrightarrow{x}F_{n}\xrightarrow{x}F_{n-1}\xrightarrow{x}\cdots.

in virtue of (
3.1.6
).
Assume that
F_{\bullet}
is pseudo-perfect.
Then
F_{i}\simeq 0
for every
i\gg 0
.

By
Proposition

2.1.12
, the
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
admits a compact generator
E
. By
Corollary

2.1.16
we have

F_{\bullet}(E)\in\Fun(\mathbb{Z}^{op},\Mod_{R})^{\omega}\ .

Thus by
Corollary

3.1.4
there exists
N\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
F_{i}(E)\simeq 0
for every
i>N
.
To conclude the proof notice that
E
generates
\mathcal{C}^{\omega}
under finite colimits retracts and shifts, so that
F_{i}(C)\simeq 0
for every
i>N
and
C\in\mathcal{C}^{\omega}
.
∎

Lemma 3.1.8
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
smooth equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
and
\Spec(R)\in\dAff_{k}
discrete.
Let
F\in\mathcal{C}_{\Theta_{R}}
and represent it by

F_{\bullet}\colon\ \ \cdots\xrightarrow{x}F_{n}\xrightarrow{x}F_{n-1}\xrightarrow{x}\cdots

in virtue of (
3.1.6
).
Assume
F
is
\tau_{\Theta_{R}}
-flat.
Then:

(a)

F_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
;

(b)

the map
F_{n}\xrightarrow{x}F_{n-1}
is a monomorphism for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
;

(c)

F_{n}/xF_{n+1}
is
\tau_{R}
-flat for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Let
p\colon\Spec(R[x])\to\Theta_{R}
be the atlas map.
By
Lemma

2.2.20
we have that
p_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet}
is
\tau_{R[x]}
-flat.
In particular
p_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet}\in\mathcal{C}^{\heartsuit}_{R[x]}
.
By definition of
\tau_{R[x]}
, we have that
p_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet}\in\mathcal{C}^{\heartsuit}_{R[x]}
if and only if the underlying family
F
over
R
is in
\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
.
By
Remark

A.1.2
the family
F
is identified with
\bigoplus_{n}F_{n}
, hence
F_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
for every
n
.
This proves (a).

By
Proposition

2.3.3
the exact sequence in
\Mod_{R[x]}^{\heartsuit}

0\to R[x]\xrightarrow{x}R[x]

is sent to an exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}_{R[x]}^{\heartsuit}

0\to p_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{x}p_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet}\ .

By definition of
\tau_{R[x]}
, the above map induces an exact sequence on the underlying families over
R

0\to\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}F_{n}\xrightarrow{x}\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}F_{n}\ .

Hence every map
x\colon F_{n}\to F_{n-1}
is a monomorphism. This proves (b).

The pullback of
F_{\bullet}
along the closed immersion
i\colon\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\Theta_{R}
is
\tau
-flat by
Lemma

2.2.20
.
The underlying
\tau
