-flat family over
R
is identified with
\bigoplus_{n}F_{n-1}/xF_{n}
by (b) and
Theorem

A.1.1
.
Since
\tau_{R}
is
\omega
-accessible, it follows that the homotopy group functors commute with arbitrary direct sums.
Hence
F_{n}/xF_{n+1}
is
\tau_{R}
-flat for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. This proves (c).
∎

Lemma 3.1.9
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
smooth equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
.
Let
R\in\Aff_{k}
.
Let
F\in\mathcal{C}_{\Theta_{R}}
and represent it by

F_{\bullet}\colon\ \ \cdots\xrightarrow{x}F_{n}\xrightarrow{x}F_{n-1}\xrightarrow{x}\cdots

in virtue of (
3.1.6
).
Assume that

(1)

for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
we have
F_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
and the map
F_{n}\xrightarrow{x}F_{n-1}
is a monomorphism;

(2)

for
n\gg 0
we have
F_{n}\simeq 0
;

(3)

for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
the family
F_{n}/xF_{n+1}
is pseudo-perfect and
\tau_{R}
-flat.

Then
F_{n}
is
\tau_{R}
-flat and pseudo-perfect for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

By (2) there exists a biggest integer
n_{0}
for which
F_{n_{0}}\neq 0
.
It follows directly by (3) that
F_{n_{0}}
is pseudo-perfect and
\tau_{R}
-flat.
We claim that
F_{n_{0}-1}
is also pseudo-perfect and
\tau_{R}
-flat; indeed by (1) it fits if an exact sequence

0\to F_{n_{0}}\to F_{{n_{0}}-1}\to F_{n_{0}-1}/xF_{n_{0}}\to 0.

Thus
F_{{n_{0}}-1}
is an extension of pseudo-perfect and
\tau_{R}
-flat families, which proves the claim.
Arguing by induction we get that
F_{n}
is pseudo-perfect and
\tau_{R}
-flat for any
n
.
∎

Notation 3.1.10
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a stable
\infty
-category.
Let
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Consider the isomorphism of poset

g_{n}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{op}\to\mathbb{Z}^{op}

m\mapsto m-n

Given
F_{\bullet}\in\Rep(\mathbb{Z},\mathcal{E})
we denote by
F_{\bullet}\langle n\rangle
composition

\mathbb{Z}^{op}\xrightarrow{g_{n}}\mathbb{Z}^{op}\xrightarrow{F_{\bullet}}\mathcal{E}\ .

Proposition 3.1.11
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
smooth equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
.
Let
R\in\Aff_{k}
.
Let
F\in\mathcal{C}_{\Theta_{R}}
and represent it by

F_{\bullet}\colon\ \ \cdots\xrightarrow{x}F_{n}\xrightarrow{x}F_{n-1}\xrightarrow{x}\cdots

via (
3.1.6
).
Then
F
is
\tau_{\Theta_{R}}
-flat and pseudo-perfect if and only if the following are satisfied:

(i)

for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
we have
F_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
and the map
F_{n}\xrightarrow{x}F_{n-1}
is a monomorphism;

(ii)

for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
the map
F_{n}\xrightarrow{x}F_{n-1}
is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
for
n\ll 0
;

(iii)

for
n\gg 0
we have
F_{n}\simeq 0
;

(iv)

for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
the family
F_{n}/xF_{n+1}
is
\tau
-flat over
R
;

(v)

for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
the family
F_{n}/xF_{n+1}
is pseudo-perfect over
R
.

Suppose
F_{\bullet}\in\mathcal{C}_{\Theta_{R}}
is
\tau_{\Theta_{R}}
-flat and pseudo-perfect.
Then (i) and (iv) follow by
Lemma

3.1.8
.
By stability under pullbacks of pseudo-perfectness, the associated graded is pseudo-perfect (
Theorem

A.1.1
-(4)).
But a direct sum can be pseudo-perfect if and only if it is finite and all the summands are pseudo-perfect.
It follows immediately that
x\colon F_{n}\to F_{n+1}
is an isomorphism for
n\ll 0
and that
F_{n}/xF_{n+1}
is pseudo-perfect for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
This proves (ii) and (v).
Condition (iii) follows by
Corollary

3.1.7
.

Assume now that
F_{\bullet}
satisfies (i), (ii), (iii) and (iv).
By
Lemma

3.1.9
the family
F_{n}
is pseudo-perfect and
\tau_{R}
flat for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
By (iii) the family
F_{n}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{O}_{\Theta_{R}}
is pseudo-perfect and
\tau
-flat over
\Theta_{R}
for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Shifting the grading by
-n
, we get the pseudo-perfect and
\tau
-flat family over
\Theta_{R}

F_{n}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{O}_{\Theta_{R}}\langle-n\rangle\colon\ \cdots\to 0\to F_{n}\xrightarrow{id}F_{n}\xrightarrow{id}F_{n}\xrightarrow{id}\cdots,

where the first non-zero element is in weight
n
.
Now the result follows since by (i), (iii) and (iv),
F_{\bullet}
is obtained as a finite number of extensions of
\tau
-flat and pseudo-perfect families.
Indeed by (iii) there exists a biggest integer
n_{0}
for which
F_{n_{0}}\neq 0
.
By (i) we have that the family

G_{\bullet}\coloneqq\cdots 0\to F_{n_{0}}\xrightarrow{x}F_{n_{0}-1}\xrightarrow{id}F_{n_{0}-1}\xrightarrow{id}\cdots

is the extension of
F\langle n_{0}\rangle
by
F_{n_{0}-1}/F_{n_{0}}\langle n_{0}-1\rangle
, i.e., we have a short exact sequence

0\to F_{n_{0}}\langle n_{0}\rangle\to F_{\bullet}^{\prime}\to F_{n_{0}-1}/F_{n_{0}}\langle n_{0}-1\rangle\to 0.

By (iv) and (v) both
F_{n_{0}}\langle n_{0}\rangle
and
F_{n_{0}-1}/F_{n_{0}}\langle n_{0}-1\rangle
are pseudo-perfect and
\tau_{\Theta_{R}}
-flat, so that
G_{\bullet}
is pseudo-perfect and
\tau_{\Theta_{R}}
-flat.
Iterating this process, which eventually stops by (ii), we get the result.
∎

Remark 3.1.12
.

In the setting of
Proposition

3.1.11
, if
F_{\bullet}
is
\tau_{\Theta_{R}}
-flat and
\tau
satisfies
3.0.1
, we can replace conditions (i)-to-(v) with conditions (i)-to-(iv) and:

(v’)

for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
the family
F_{n}
is pseudo-perfect over
R
.

Indeed combining (i) with
3.0.1
we get that (v’) implies (v).
The converse holds by
Lemma

3.1.9
.

Proposition 3.1.13
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
of finite type equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
satisfying
3.0.1
and universally satisfying openness of flatness.
The algebraic stack
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
is
\Theta
-reductive.

Consider a pseudo-perfect
\tau_{\Theta_{R}}
-flat family
F\in\mathcal{C}_{\Theta_{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}}
corresponding to a map

\Theta_{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}\xrightarrow{F}\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}.

We need to verify that the unique
\tau_{\Theta_{R}}
-flat extension
j_{\ast}F\in\mathcal{C}_{\Theta_{R}}
provided by
Lemma

2.3.16
is pseudo-perfect.
By
Lemma

3.1.8
, it is enough to to verify (ii), (iii) and (v) of
Proposition

3.1.11
.
By
Remark

3.1.12
, we can replace (v) by (v’) in
loc.cit.
.
The family
F
can be described as:

•

a family
F\in\mathcal{C}_{\Frac(R)}
with a filtration

F_{\bullet}\colon\ \cdots\subseteq F_{n}\subseteq F_{n-1}\subseteq\cdots

satisfying the conditions of
Proposition

3.1.11
;

•

A pseudo-perfect
\tau
-flat
R
-module subobject
E_{1}\subseteq F
with an isomorphism

E_{1}\otimes_{R}\Frac(R)\simeq F\ .

Then the unique
\tau_{\Theta_{R}}
-flat extension can be explicitly described as in
[
AHLH23
, Lemma 7.17]
.
That is, it is the filtered object

j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F\simeq F_{\bullet}\cap E_{1}=(\cdots\xrightarrow{x}F_{n}\cap E_{1}\xrightarrow{x}F_{n-1}\cap E_{1}\xrightarrow{x}\cdots)\hookrightarrow(\cdots\xhookrightarrow{x}F_{n}\xhookrightarrow{x}F_{n-1}\xhookrightarrow{x}\cdots)=F_{\bullet}\ .

Since
E_{1}
is pseudo-perfect and this properties is stable under taking subobjects by
3.0.1
, we get that
F_{n}\cap E_{1}
is pseudo-perfect for any
n
.
Since the filtration of
F
stabilizes for
n\ll 0
and
F_{n}\simeq 0
for
n\gg 0
, the same holds true for
F_{\bullet}\cap E_{1}
.
Thus
j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F
satisfies (i)-to-(v) of
Proposition

3.1.11
, hence defining an unique extension

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Theta_{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Theta_{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!\ j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F}

∎

Proposition

3.1.11
also has the following immediate corollary:

Corollary 3.1.14

(
[
AHLH23
, Lemma 7.19]
)
.

In the setting of
Proposition

3.1.13
, let
\kappa
be a field over
k
and
F\in\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}^{\heartsuit}
be a pseudo-perfect family corresponding to a point
x_{F}\colon\Spec(\kappa)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
.
If
x_{F}
is a closed point, then
F\in\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}^{\heartsuit}
is semisimple.

Notice that since
\kappa
is a field, the family
F
is
\tau_{\kappa}
-flat by
Remark

2.2.6
.
The stack
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
has affine stabilizer by
Lemma

2.4.6
.
Since
F
is pseudo-perfect, it can not be expressed as an infinite direct sum of non-zero objects.
Then the proof of
[
AHLH23
, Lemma 7.19]
goes trough without changes.
∎

3.2.
\mathrm{S}
-completeness

We will prove in this section that the moduli of objects
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
is
\mathrm{S}
-complete.
Recall that
R
is a DVR with uniformizer
\pi
, residue field
\kappa
and fraction field
K
.
In order to proceed with this verification, we will first characterize explicitly
\tau_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
-flat pseudo-perfect families in
\mathcal{C}_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
in
Proposition

3.2.6
.
We recall in the next paragraph the main results of Appendix
A
.

Recollection 3.2.1
.

The following derived analogue of
[
AHLH23
, Lemma 7.14]
hold:

•

Objects of
\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})
are identified with

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

where the
F_{i}
’s are
R
-modules and
t,s
are maps of
R
-modules that satisfy
st=\pi=ts
.
Moreover under this identification, an object is (co)connective if and only if every
F_{n}
is.

•

Restriction along the open immersion
\Spec(R)\xhookrightarrow{s\neq 0}ST
induces the functor

Restriction along the closed immersion
\Theta_{\kappa}\xhookrightarrow{s=0}\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}
induces the functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cdots\xrightarrow{t}F_{n}/sF_{n-1}\xrightarrow{t}F_{n-1}/sF_{n-2}\xrightarrow{t}\cdots}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

where the quotient has to be understood as cofibers along the morphisms
s
.

Restriction along the closed immersion
\Theta_{\kappa}\xhookrightarrow{t=0}\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}
induces the functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\cdots\xleftarrow{s}F_{n}/tF_{n+1}\xleftarrow{s}F_{n-1}/tF_{n}\xleftarrow{s}\cdots}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

where the quotient has to be understood as cofibers along the morphisms
t
.

Recollection 3.2.2
.

Via the equivalence of
Corollary

A.1.8
and
Remark

A.1.9
, we can represent an object
F_{\bullet}\in\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})
by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

where
F_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{R}
for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Let
i_{\left\{s=0\right\}}\colon\Theta_{\kappa}\to\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}
be the closed immersion corresponding to the locus
\left\{s=0\right\}
.
By
Proposition

A.2.2
, the pullback along
i_{\left\{s=0\right\}}
is identified with

\Gr_{s}\colon\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})\to\QCoh(\Theta_{\kappa})

Lemma 3.2.3
.

Let
M\in\Mod_{\kappa}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
i_{n}\colon\left\{n\right\}\to\mathbb{Z}
be the inclusion and
\ev_{n,!}
be the left adjoint to the evaluation functor
\ev_{n}\coloneqq i_{n}^{\ast}\colon\Fun(\mathbb{Z};\Mod_{\kappa})\to\Mod_{\kappa}
.
In the setting of
3.2.2
, consider the quasi-coherent complex on
\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}

where the first non-zero copy of
M
is placed in weight
n
.
If
M\in\Perf(\kappa)
, then
M(\leq n)\in\Perf(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})
.

In the adjunction

\fib_{s}^{\mathrm{L}}\circ\ev_{n,!}\dashv ev_{n}\circ\fib_{s}

the right adjoint preserves filtered colimits as both
ev_{n}
and
\fib_{s}
do.
It then follows by
[
Lur09
, Lemma 5.5.14]
that
\fib_{s}^{\mathrm{L}}\circ\ev_{n,!}
preserves compact objects.
In particular, if
M\in\Perf(\kappa)=\Mod_{\kappa}^{\omega}
, then
M(\leq n)\in\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})^{\omega}
.
Since we are in characteristic zero, we also have
\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})^{\omega}=\Perf(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})
by
[
BZFN10
, Corollary 3.22]
. Hence
M(\leq n)
is perfect.
∎

Lemma 3.2.4
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
smooth equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
.
Let
F_{\bullet}\in\mathcal{C}_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
and represent it by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

in virtue of
3.2.2
.
Assume that

(a)

F_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
;

(b)

t\colon F_{n}\to F_{n-1}
is a monomorphism for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
;

(c)

s\colon F_{n}\to F_{n+1}
is an isomorphism for
n\gg 0
and
t\colon F_{n}\to F_{n-1}
is an isomorphism
n\ll 0
;

(d)

F_{n}
is pseudo-perfect over
R
for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Then
F_{\bullet}
is pseudo-perfect over
\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}
.

By (c) there exists two integers
n_{1}\geq n_{2}
such that
s\colon F_{n}\to F_{n+1}
is an isomorphism for every
n\geq n_{1}
and
t\colon F_{n}\to F_{n-1}
is an isomorphism for every
n\leq n_{2}
. We will proceed by induction on
r=n_{1}-n_{2}
.
If
r=0
then
F_{\bullet}
is equivalent to

(F_{n_{2}})_{\bullet}\langle n_{2}\rangle\colon\ \hbox to255.56pt{\vbox to23.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 127.776pt\lower-11.52773pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
