where the central copy of
F_{n_{2}}
sits in weight
n_{2}
.
Let

p\colon\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))\to\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}

be the atlas map.
Invoking
Lemma

2.2.21
it is enough to show that
(F_{n_{2}})_{\bullet}\langle{n_{2}}\rangle
is pseudo-perfect over
\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}
.
By
Corollary

A.1.11
, the pullback
p_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}(F_{n_{2}})_{\bullet}\langle{n_{2}}\rangle
is identified with (a shift of)

\widetilde{F}_{n_{2}}\coloneqq F_{n_{2}}\otimes_{R}(R[s,t]/(st-\pi)).

Hence, it is enough to show that
\widetilde{F}_{n_{2}}
is pseudo-perfect over
\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))
.
This follows immediately from (d) and
Lemma

2.2.20
.
Suppose now that the statement holds for
r-1=n_{1}-n_{2}-1
.
Then
F_{\bullet}
is equivalent to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G_{\bullet}\colon\ \ \ \cdots}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{M+2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{M+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

Consider the following sequence in
\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H_{\bullet}\colon\ \ \ \ \cdots}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n_{2}+2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n_{2}+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n_{2}+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n_{2}+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G_{\bullet}\colon\ \ \ \cdots}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n_{2}+2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n_{2}+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n_{2}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n_{2}}/tF_{n_{2}+1}(\leq n_{2})\colon\ \cdots}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n_{2}}/tF_{n_{2}+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n_{2}}/tF_{n_{2}+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

By (b) the above is a fiber sequence in
\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})
.
Hence it is enough to show that
H_{\bullet}
and
F_{n_{2}}/tF_{n_{2}+1}(\leq n_{2})
are pseudo-perfect.
The former is pseudo-perfect by recursion.
By (b) and (d), the quotient
F_{n_{2}}/tF_{n_{2}+1}
is pseudo-perfect over
R
.
Moreover the action of
\pi
on
F_{n_{2}}/tF_{n_{2}+1}\in\mathcal{C}_{R}\simeq\Fun^{ex}((\mathcal{C}^{\omega})^{op},\Mod_{R})
is trivial as
\pi=ts
.
It follows that
F_{n_{2}}/tF_{n_{2}+1}
is (the pushforward of an element) in
\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}\simeq\Fun^{ex}((\mathcal{C}^{\omega})^{op},\Mod_{\kappa})
, and it is automatically pseudo-perfect over
\kappa
.
By
Lemma

3.2.3
, the family
F_{n_{2}}/tF_{n_{2}+1}(\leq n_{2})
is pseudo-perfect over
\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}
.
∎

Lemma 3.2.5
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
smooth equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
.
Let
F_{\bullet}\in\mathcal{C}_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
and represent it by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

in virtue of
3.2.2
.
Then
F
is
\tau_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
-flat if and only if the following are satisfied:

(i)

for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have
F_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
and the maps
F_{n}\xrightarrow{t}F_{n-1},F_{n}\xrightarrow{s}F_{n+1}
are monomorphisms;

(ii)

for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the map
F_{n+1}/sF_{n}\xrightarrow{t}F_{n}/sF_{n-1}
is a monomorphism.

Also, condition (ii) can be replaced by

(ii’)

for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the map
F_{n-1}/tF_{n+1}\xrightarrow{s}F_{n+1}/tF_{n}
is a monomorphism for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Assume
F\in\mathcal{C}_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
is
\tau_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
-flat.
Arguing as in the proof of
Lemma

3.1.8
and using
Remark

A.1.10
, item (i) follows.
Similarly using
3.2.1
, item (ii) and (ii’) follow.
But then by
Proposition

3.1.11
-(iii) we have that
F_{n+1}/sF_{n}\simeq 0
for
n\gg 0
and
F_{n}/tF_{n-1}\simeq 0
for
n\ll 0
.

Conversely, suppose that
F_{\bullet}\in\mathcal{C}_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
satisfies (i) and (ii).
Then (i) shows that multiplication by
\pi
is injective on
(F_{\bullet})|_{\left\{s\neq 0\right\}}
and
(F_{\bullet})|_{{}_{\left\{t\neq 0\right\}}}
.
Hence
(F_{\bullet})|_{\left\{s\neq 0\right\}}
and
(F_{\bullet})|_{{}_{\left\{t\neq 0\right\}}}
are
\tau
-flat over
(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})_{s\neq 0}\simeq\Spec(R)\simeq(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})_{t\neq 0}
by
Corollary

2.3.6
.
By (ii) we have that multiplication by
t
is injective on
(F_{\bullet})|_{\left\{t=0\right\}}
, which in turn implies that
(F_{\bullet})
is
\tau
-flat at the origin by
Lemma

A.2.6
.
A similar argument shows that (i) and (ii’) also characterize flatness.
∎

Proposition 3.2.6
.

In the setting of
3.0.1
, let
F_{\bullet}\in\mathcal{C}_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
and represent it by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F_{n-1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

in virtue of
3.2.2
.
Assume
F_{\bullet}
is
\tau_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
-flat. Then
F_{\bullet}
is pseudo-perfect if and only if:

(i)

for every
n\gg 0
, the map
s\colon F_{n}\to F_{n+1}
is an isomorphism and for every
n\ll 0
, the map
t\colon F_{n}\to F_{n-1}
is an isomorphism;

(ii)

for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the family
F_{n}
is pseudo-perfect over
R
.

Assume that
F_{\bullet}
is pseudo-perfect.
The restriction to
s=0
and
t=0
are pseudo-perfect and
\tau_{\Theta_{\kappa}}
-flat.
By
Proposition

3.1.11
-(iii) and
3.2.1
, we get
F_{n+1}/sF_{n}\simeq 0
for
n\gg 0
and
F_{n}/tF_{n-1}\simeq 0
for
n\ll 0
.
This proves (iii).
To get (ii) one notices that by (i) we have that there exists
N\ll 0
such that

(i_{\left\{t\neq 0\right\}})_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet}\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{colim}(\cdots\xrightarrow{t}F_{n}\xrightarrow{t}F_{n-1}\xrightarrow{t}\cdots)\simeq F_{N}.

Thus
F_{N}
is pseudo-perfect and
\tau
-flat over
R
.
By
Proposition

3.2.6
-(i) and
3.0.1
, the family
F_{n}
is pseudo-perfect for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Conversely, assume that
F_{\bullet}
satisfies (i) and (ii).
Since
F_{\bullet}
is
\tau_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
-flat,
Lemma

3.2.5
implies that the conditions of
Lemma

3.2.4
are satisfied.
The conclusion follows.
∎

Proposition 3.2.7
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
of finite type equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
satisfying
3.0.1
and universally satisfying openness of flatness.
The algebraic stack
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
is
\mathrm{S}
-complete.

Consider a
\tau
-flat pseudo-perfect family
F_{\bullet}\in\mathcal{C}_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}}
corresponding to a map

\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}\xrightarrow{F}\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}.

We need to verify that the unique
\tau_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
-flat extension
\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F\in\mathcal{C}_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
provided by
Lemma

2.3.16
is pseudo-perfect. By
Lemma

A.2.4
, the family
F_{\bullet}
can be described as:

•

a family
F\in\mathcal{C}_{\Frac(R)}^{\heartsuit}
;

•

pseudo-perfect
\tau
-flat
R
-module subobjects
E_{1},E_{2}\subseteq F
with equivalences

E_{1}\otimes_{R}\Frac(R)\simeq F\simeq E_{2}\otimes_{R}\Frac(R)\ .

Moreover,
Corollary

A.2.5
shows that the unique
\tau_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
-flat extension can be explicitly described by

\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F=\bigoplus_{n}(E_{1}\cap(\pi^{-n}E_{2}))t^{n}\subseteq\bigoplus_{n}Ft^{n},

Since
E_{1}
is pseudo-perfect and
E_{1}\cap(\pi^{-n}E_{2})
is a subobject of
E_{1}
, each graded piece is pseudo-perfect by
3.0.1
.
Since

F\simeq E_{2}\otimes_{R}K\simeq E_{2}\otimes_{R}\operatornamewithlimits{colim}(\cdots\xrightarrow{\pi}R\xrightarrow{\pi}R\xrightarrow{\pi}\cdots),

the union of the filtration

\cdots\subseteq E_{1}\cap(\pi^{-n}E_{2})\subseteq E_{1}\cap(\pi^{-n-1}E_{2})\subseteq\cdots

is
E_{1}\cap(E_{2}\otimes_{R}K)\simeq E_{1}\cap F=E_{1}
.
Because
E_{1}
is pseudo-perfect (thus compact by
Lemma

2.1.17
), this union must stabilize.
Similarly by inverting the roles of
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
, the filtration also stabilizes on the left.
Thus
\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F
satisfies (i)-(ii) of
Proposition

3.2.6
, hence defining an unique extension

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!\ \pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F}

∎

Proposition

3.2.7
allows us to characterize closed points of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
. Indeed we show in
Corollary

3.2.11
that closed points are in bijection with semisimple objects.

Lemma 3.2.8
.

Let
\kappa
be a field of characteristic zero and
\mathcal{X}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Y}
be an immersion in
\St_{\kappa}
.

(1)

If
\mathcal{Y}
is
\mathrm{S}
-complete,
\mathcal{X}
is of finite type and
\kappa
is algebraically closed, then any specialization of
\kappa
-points
x\rightsquigarrow y
in
\mathcal{X}
, where
y
is closed in
\mathcal{X}
, is realized by a morphism
\Theta_{\kappa}\to\mathcal{X}
.

(2)

If
\mathcal{Y}
is
\Theta
-reductive and
\mathrm{S}
-complete, then the closure of any
\kappa
-point of
\mathcal{Y}
contains a unique closed point.

Since immersions induces equivalence between automorphisms groups of points and we are in characteristic zero, it follows from
Lemma

2.4.6
,
Proposition

3.2.7
and
[
AHLH23
, Remark 2.6 & Proposition 3.47]
that we can apply
[
AHLH23
, Lemma 3.24 & Lemma 3.25]
.
∎

Recollection 3.2.9
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
be of finite type equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
universally satisfying openness of flatness.
Let
E\in\mathcal{C}
be a compact generator and
\nu:\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{N}
be a function with finite support.
Define a presheaf

\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\nu}:\dAff_{k}^{op}\to\Spc

that assigns to
\Spec(A)
the maximal
\infty
-subgroupoid

\mathcal{C}^{\nu}(A)\subset(C_{A}^{pp})^{\simeq}

spanned by those
F\in\Fun^{st}((\mathcal{C}^{\omega})^{op},\Perf(A))
for which the following property holds: for any field
K
and any
A\to\pi_{0}(A)\to K
we have
\dim_{K}\pi_{i}(F(E)\otimes_{A}K)\leq\nu(i)
.
This defines a substack of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}
that depends on the choice of
nu
and of the compact generator
E
.
By
[
TV07
, Proposition 3.20]
, the derived stack
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\nu}
is an open substack of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}
of finite presentation.
We denote by
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0],\nu}
the intersection of the open substacks
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}
and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\nu}
in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}
, and by
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\nu}
its truncation.
Since
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\nu}\subset t_{0}\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\nu}
is an open substack of a finitely presented stack over the noetherian ring
k
, it follows that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\nu}
is locally of finite presentation and quasi-compact.
Hence
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\nu}
is of finite presentation.

Lemma 3.2.10
.

In the setting of
3.2.9
, let
\kappa
be a field over
k
and
x,y\colon\Spec(\kappa)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
.
There exists a function
\nu\colon\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{N}
with finite support such that both
x
and
y
factor through
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\nu}
.

Let
F,G\in\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}^{\heartsuit}\subset\Fun^{st}((\mathcal{C}^{\omega})^{op},\Perf(\kappa))
be the pseudo-perfect objects corresponding respectively to
x,y
.
For
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, define
h^{i}(F)\coloneqq\dim_{\kappa}H^{i}(F(E))
and define
h^{i}(G)
similarly.
Since
F(E),G(E)\in\Perf(\kappa)
, the function

\nu\colon\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{N}

i\mapsto\mathrm{max}\left\{h^{i}(F),h^{i}(G)\right\}

has finite support.
By definition of
\nu
, the morphisms
x,y\colon\Spec(\kappa)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
factor through
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\nu}
.
∎

Corollary 3.2.11
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
of finite type equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
satisfying
3.0.1
and universally satisfying openness of flatness.
Let
\Spec(\kappa)\to\Spec(k)
be a closed point.
There is a bijection between closed
\kappa
-points of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
and pseudo-perfect semisimple objects in
\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}^{\heartsuit}
.

Since the (closed)
\kappa
-points of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
are in bijection with the (closed)
\kappa
-points of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}\times_{\Spec(k)}\Spec(\kappa)
, we can assume that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}\in\St_{\kappa}
.
Notice that since
\kappa
is a field, any family in
\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}^{\heartsuit}
is
\tau_{\kappa}
-flat (
Remark

2.2.6
).
Hence every pseudo-perfect family
F\in\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}^{\heartsuit}
corresponds uniquely to a morphism
x_{F}\colon\Spec(\kappa)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
.

By
Corollary

3.1.14
, any closed
\kappa
-point must parametrize a pseudo-perfect semisimple object of
\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}^{\heartsuit}
.
Let now
F\in\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}^{\heartsuit}
be a pseudo-perfect semisimple object and
x_{F}\colon\Spec(\kappa)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
be the corresponding morphism.
By
Lemma

3.2.8
-(2), the closure of
x_{F}
contains a unique closed point
x_{G}
.
Up to replace
\kappa
with an extension, we can suppose that
\kappa
is algebraically closed and that
x_{F},x_{G}
are
\kappa
-rational.
Let
G\in C^{\heartsuit}_{\kappa}
be the pseudo-perfect family corresponding to
x_{G}
.
We wish to show that
x_{F}
and
x_{G}
agree, that is
F\simeq G
.
By
Lemma

3.2.10
, there exists a function
\nu\colon\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{N}
with finite support such that
x_{F},x_{G}
factor through
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\nu}
.
By
Lemma

3.2.8
-(1) and
3.2.9
, the specialization
x_{F}\rightsquigarrow x_{G}
in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\nu}
is realized by a morphism
\Theta_{\kappa}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\nu}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
.
Explicitly this means that
G
is identified with the associated graded of a filtration of
F
(
Proposition

3.1.11
and
Theorem

A.1.1
-(4)).
Since
F
is supposed semisimple, it follows that
F\simeq G
.
∎

3.3.
Existence of good moduli spaces for
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}

We know put all the results together to obtain the main statement of the article.

Definition 3.3.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
be of finite type equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
.
Let
A
be a discrete commutative algebra over
k
and
F\in\mathcal{C}^{\heartsuit}_{A}
be a pseudo-perfect
\tau_{A}
-flat object.
We say that
F
lies over a substack
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
if the associated morphism
x_{F}\colon\Spec(A)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
factors through
\mathcal{X}
.

For
\Spec(\kappa)\to\Spec(k)
a closed point with
\kappa
algebraically closed, we have the following

Theorem 3.3.2
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
of finite type equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
satisfying
3.0.1
and universally satisfying openness of flatness.
Then
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
is
\Theta
-reductive and
\mathrm{S}
-complete.
In particular, any closed quasi-compact substack
\mathcal{X}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
admits a separated good moduli space
X
whose
\kappa
-points parametrize pseudo-perfect semisimple objects of
\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}^{\heartsuit}
lying over
\mathcal{X}
.
Moreover,
X
admits a natural derived enhancement if
\mathcal{X}
is the truncation of a derived substack of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}
.

By
Proposition

3.1.13
and
Proposition

3.2.7
,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
is
\Theta
-reductive and
\mathrm{S}
-complete.
Moreover
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
has affine diagonal by
Lemma

2.4.6
.
Since this properties are preserved under taking closed substacks, the hypothesis of
Theorem

2.4.5
are satisfied for
\mathcal{X}
.
The characterization of
\kappa
-points of
X
follows from
Theorem

2.4.2
-(4) and
Corollary

3.2.11
.
The natural derived enhancement is provided by
Theorem

2.4.8
.
∎

Corollary 3.3.3
.

In the setting of
Theorem

3.3.2
, assume that the connected components of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
are quasi-compact.
Then
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
admits a separated good moduli space
\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
.
Moreover, the
\kappa
-points of
\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
parametrize pseudo-perfect semisimple objects of
\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}^{\heartsuit}
.
Furthermore,
\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
admits a natural a derived enhancement
\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}
which is a derived good moduli space for
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}
.

By
[
Alp13
, Proposition 4.7-(ii)]
, it is enough to prove that every connected component of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
admits a separated good moduli space.
The claims then follow at once by our assumption that the connected components of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
are quasi-compact and
Theorem

3.3.2
. ∎

In the next paragraph, we give applications of
Theorem

3.3.2
.

3.3.1.
Quasi-compact substacks of extensions

In this subsection we want to prove that there are natural closed substacks of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
that are quasi-compact.
This substacks roughly arise by fixing a finite number of objects in
\mathcal{C}^{\heartsuit}
and looking at all elements that are extensions of the given objects.

Recollection 3.3.4
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
of finite type equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
universally satisfying openness of flatness.
Following
[
Por25
, Section I.5.6]
, we introduce the derived stack of extensions
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0],ext}
.
We will not give the precise construction, but we will limit ourselves to describe its points: a morphism
\Spec(A)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0],ext}
corresponds to a fiber/cofiber sequence

F_{1}\to F_{2}\to F_{3}

with the
F_{i}
’s pseudo-perfect and
\tau_{A}
-flat.
Notice that if
\Spec(A)
is underived, the above is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}_{A}^{\heartsuit}
.
The stack
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0],ext}
comes with a morphism

(\ev_{1},\ev_{2},\ev_{3})\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0],ext}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}

that sends a fiber/cofiber sequence to its components.

Notation 3.3.5
.

We will denote by
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,ext}
the classical truncation of the derived stack
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0],ext}
.

Proposition 3.3.6
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
of finite type equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
satisfying
3.0.1
and universally satisfying openness of flatness.
The morphism of stacks over
k

\ev_{2}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,ext}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}

(0\to F_{1}\to F_{2}\to F_{3}\to 0)\mapsto F_{2}

satisfies the valuative criterion for properness.

Let
R
be a DVR and
K
be its fraction field.
Consider the lifting problem

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spec(K)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,ext}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spec(R)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

We want to prove that a lift exist and it is unique.
The above diagram corresponds to a short exact sequence

(3.3.7)

0\to F_{1}\to\widetilde{F}_{2}\otimes_{R}K\to F_{3}\to 0

in
\mathcal{C}_{K}^{\heartsuit}
, where
F_{1},F_{3}
are pseudo-perfect
\tau
-flat families over
K
, and
F_{2}
is a pseudo-perfect
\tau
-flat families over
R
.
In virtue of
2.2.4
we can see (
3.3.7
) as a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
.
Notice that the families in (
3.3.7
) are not necessarily pseudo-perfect over
R
.
Define
\widetilde{F}_{3}
to be the image of the composite map
\widetilde{F}_{2}\to\widetilde{F}_{2}\otimes_{R}K\to F_{3}
.
By construction
\widetilde{F}_{3}
is a quotient of
\widetilde{F}_{2}
.
Hence
\widetilde{F}_{3}
is pseudo-perfect over
R
by
3.0.1
.
Since
F_{3}
is
\tau_{R}
-flat and
\widetilde{F}_{3}
is a subobject of
F_{3}
, it follows from
2.3.6
that
\widetilde{F}_{3}
is
\tau_{R}
-flat.
By the two-out-of-three property for perfect complexes, it follows that

\widetilde{F}_{1}\coloneqq\mathrm{Ker}(\widetilde{F}_{2}\to\widetilde{F}_{3})\simeq\mathrm{fib}(\widetilde{F}_{2}\to\widetilde{F}_{3})

is pseudo-perfect.
Since
\widetilde{F}_{1}
is a subobject of
\widetilde{F}_{2}
, it follows again from
Corollary

2.3.6
that
\widetilde{F}_{1}
is also
\tau_{R}
-flat.
Thus we have constructed a lift

0\to\widetilde{F}_{1}\to\widetilde{F}_{2}\to\widetilde{F}_{3}\to 0

of (
3.3.7
).
The uniqueness follows by the uniqueness of the epi-mono factorization.
∎

We will also need the following lemma:

Lemma 3.3.8
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
of finite type equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
universally satisfying openness of flatness.
There exists a perfect complex
\mathcal{E}\in\Perf(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]})
and a commutative

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0],ext}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ev_{1},\ev_{3})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

where
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E})\coloneqq\Spec(\mathrm{Sym}(\mathcal{E}))
and
\phi
is an equivalence.
In particular
(ev_{1},ev_{3})
is a quasi-compact morphism.

Remark 3.3.9
.

Lemma

3.3.8
should not be surprising, indeed above any point parametrizing a pair of objects
F_{1},F_{3}
, the (connected components of) fiber of
(\ev_{1},\ev_{3})
consists on the possible extensions
\Ext^{1}(F_{1},F_{3})
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be the universal family of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}
, i.e., the
\tau
-flat pseudo-perfect family

\mathcal{F}\in\Fun((\mathcal{C}^{\omega})^{op},\Perf(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}))

corresponding to the identity of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}
.
Let
p_{i}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}
be the canonical projections and set
\mathcal{F}_{i}\coloneqq p_{i}^{\ast}\mathcal{F}
.
By definition,
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}
are pseudo-perfect.
Thus

\mathcal{E}\coloneqq\Hom(\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2})^{\vee}[-1]\in\Perf(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]})

by
Corollary

2.1.24
and we can define the linear stack over
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}
associated to
\mathcal{E}
:

\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E})\coloneqq\Spec(\mathrm{Sym}(\mathcal{E})).

For
x\colon\Spec(A)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}
parametrizing a couple of pseudo-perfect
\tau_{A}
-flat families
F_{1},F_{2}
, we have

(3.3.10)

\displaystyle\Map_{\big/(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]})}

\displaystyle(\Spec(A),\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E}))\simeq\tau_{\geq 0}\Hom_{\Mod_{A}}(x^{\ast}\mathcal{E},A)\simeq

\displaystyle\simeq\tau_{\geq 0}\Hom_{\Mod_{A}}(\Hom(F_{1},F_{2})^{\vee}[-1],A)\simeq

\displaystyle\simeq\tau_{\geq 0}\Hom_{\Mod_{A}}(A,\Hom(F_{1},F_{2})[1])\simeq

\displaystyle\simeq\tau_{\geq 0}(\Hom(F_{1},F_{2})[1]),

where we used that
x^{\ast}\mathcal{E}\simeq\Hom(x^{\ast}\mathcal{F}_{1},x^{\ast}\mathcal{F}_{2})
(see
[
Por25
, Lemma I.4.9]
).
In particular, any point of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{ext,[0,0]}
defines a point of
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E})
, so that we have an induced morphism
\phi\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{ext,[0,0]}\to\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E})
.
This morphism induces an equivalence

\Map_{\big/(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]})}(\Spec(A),\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{ext,[0,0]})\to\Map_{\big/(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]})}(\Spec(A),\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E}))

for any
\Spec(A)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}
as shown by (
3.3.10
).
Thus we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0],ext}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{E})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{[0,0]}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ev_{1},\ev_{3})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

where the horizontal arrow is an equivalence, proving the statement.
∎

Construction 3.3.11
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
of finite type equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
satisfying
3.0.1
and universally satisfying openness of flatness.
Let
\Spec(\kappa)\to\Spec(k)
be a closed point with
\kappa
algebraically closed and let
r,N\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\underline{F}=\left\{F_{1},\cdots F_{r}\right\}\subset\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}^{\heartsuit}
be a finite set of pseudo-perfect semisimple objects.
Notice that the objects in
\underline{F}
are automatically
\tau_{\kappa}
-flat (
Remark

2.2.6
) and that the corresponding points of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
are closed (
Corollary

3.2.11
).
Denote by
\mathcal{G}_{F_{i}}
the associated residual gerbes and let
q_{i}\colon\mathcal{G}_{F_{i}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
be the closed immersion (
[
Sta25
,
Lemma 0H27
]
). By
[
LMB00
, Théorème 11.3]
, the stacks
\mathcal{G}_{F_{i}}
are of finite type over a field.
Consider the diagram with pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,ext,(F_{n_{1}},F_{n_{2}})}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,ext}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{G}_{F_{n_{1}}}\times\mathcal{G}_{F_{n_{2}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{2}^{(2)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ev_{1},\ev_{3})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(q_{1},q_{2})}

The projection
p
is quasi-compact as it is the pullback of a quasi-compact morphism (
Lemma

3.3.8
).
Hence
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,ext,(F_{n_{1}},F_{n_{2}})}
is an algebraic stack of finite presentation.
Since
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
has affine diagonal by
Lemma

2.4.6
, it follows from
[
Sta25
,
Lemma 075S
]
that the map
\ev_{2}^{(2)}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,ext,(F_{n_{1}},F_{n_{2}})}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
is quasi-compact.
We then define the closed substack

\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,(F_{n_{1}},F_{n_{2}})}\xhookrightarrow{i^{(2)}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}

to be the scheme-theoretic image of
\ev_{2}^{\prime}
(this is well defined by
[
Sta25
,
Lemma 081I
]
).
Since
q^{\prime}
is the pullback of a closed immersion, it is proper.
Since moreover
\ev_{2}
satisfies the valuative criterion for properness (
Proposition

3.3.6
), it follows that
\ev_{2}^{(2)}
is a quasi-compact morphism satisfying the valuative criterion for properness, thus it is proper.
In particular
\ev_{2}^{(2)}
is (universally) closed. Hence
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,(F_{n_{1}},F_{n_{2}})}
is quasi-compact by
[
Sta25
,
Lemma 02JQ
]
.

We then proceed by induction.
Suppose we have defined the closed substack

i^{(s-1)}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\underline{F}^{\prime\prime}}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}

where
F^{\prime\prime}
is the ordered set
(F_{n_{1}},\cdots F_{n_{s-1}})
. We define the closed substack
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\underline{F}^{\prime}}
to be the image of the morphism
\ev_{2}^{(s)}
defined by the diagram with pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\underline{F}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,ext}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\underline{F}^{\prime\prime}}\times\mathcal{G}_{F_{n_{s}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{2}^{(s)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ev_{1},\ev_{3})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{\prime},q_{3})}

The same proof as above shows that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\underline{F}^{\prime}}
is a well-defined quasi-compact closed substack of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
. Finally, we define the substack

\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\underline{F},N}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}

to be the union of the
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\underline{F}^{\prime}}
constructed above.

Remark 3.3.12
.

In the setting of
3.3.11
, the stack
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\underline{F},N}
is a finite union of quasi-compact closed substack of
\ \mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
.
Hence
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\underline{F},N}
is itself a quasi-compact closed substack of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
.
Moreover, its closed
\kappa
-points are in bijection with families of
\mathcal{C}_{\kappa}^{\heartsuit}
that are successive extensions of length at most
N
of elements of
\underline{F}
.

Remark 3.3.13
.

In
3.3.11
we suppose that
\kappa
is algebraically closed field only to ensure that semisimple objects corresponds to closed points of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
.
If we only suppose that
\kappa
is a field, we should ask that the objects of
\underline{F}
define closed points of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit}
. Note that a closed point must corresponds to a semisimple pseudo-perfect object by
Corollary

3.1.14
.

Theorem 3.3.14
.

In the setting of
3.3.11
, the stack
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\underline{F},N}
admits a separated good moduli space
\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\underline{F},N}
. Moreover, the
\kappa
-points of
\mathrm{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\heartsuit,\underline{F},N}
are in bijection with direct sum of at most
N
objects in
\underline{F}
, possibly with repetitions.

The statement follows by combining
Remark

3.3.12
with
Theorem

3.3.2
and
Corollary

3.2.11
.
∎

4.
Good moduli spaces for perverse sheaves

In this section we specialize the results of
Section

3.3
to the moduli of perverse sheaves.
We start our discussion by recalling the definition of perverse sheaves following
[
BBDG18
]
and the representability results from
[
PT22
,
HPT24
]
.

Notation 4.0.1
.

Throughout this section we will work with hypersheaves instead of sheaves.
In the application we are interested in, there is no difference between the two notions.
Since there is no risk of confusion, we will not make any reference to this in our notation.

Definition 4.0.2
.

A stratified space is the data of a continuous map
\rho\colon X\to P
where
X
is a topological space and
P
is a poset endowed with the Alexandroff topology.
A morphism of stratified spaces
(f,r)\colon(X,P)\to(Y,Q)
is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r}

Abusing notations, we will often refer to a stratified space as a pair
(X,P)
.
For
p\in P
, the
p
-stratum of
X
is
X_{p}\coloneqq\rho^{-1}(p)\subset X
.

Definition 4.0.3

(
[
Lur17
, Definition A.5.3]
)
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space.
The cone
(C(X),P\sqcup\left\{-\infty\right\}
) is the stratified space defined by:

(1)

as a set,
C(X)=\left\{\ast\right\}\sqcup(X\times\mathbb{R}_{>0})
;

(2)

a subset
U\subset C(X)
is open if and only if
U\cap X\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}
is open, and if
\ast\in U
then there exist
\epsilon>0
such that
X\times(0,\epsilon)\subset U
;

(3)

the stratification of
C(X)
is given by the map
\overline{\rho}\colon X\to P\sqcup\left\{-\infty\right\}
defined by
\overline{\rho}(\ast)=-\infty
and
\overline{\rho}(x,t)=\rho(x)
for
(x,t)\in X\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.

Definition 4.0.4

(
[
Lur17
, Definition A.5.5]
)
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space.
We say that
(X,P)
is conically stratified if for every
p\in P
and
x\in X_{p}
, there exists a stratified space
(Y,P_{>p})
and a topological space
Z
such that there exists an open immersion
j\colon C(Y)\times Z\to X
for which
x
lies in the image of
j
and the following diagram commute:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C(Y)\times Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C(Y)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{\geq p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\rho}_{Y}}

Definition 4.0.5
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space.
We say that
(X,P)
is conically refineable if there exists a conical stratification
(X,Q)
with locally weakly contractible strata equipped with a surjective map of posets
r\colon Q\to P
such that
(id_{X},r)\colon(X,Q)\to(X,P)
is a morphism of stratified spaces.

Definition 4.0.6
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space and let
\mathcal{E}\in\Cat_{\infty}
.
For
p\in P
, denote by
i_{p}\colon X_{p}\to X
the inclusion.
An object
F\in\Sh(X;\mathcal{E})
is constructible if
i_{p}^{\ast}F
is locally constant for every
p\in P
.
We denote by

\Cons_{P}(X;\mathcal{E})\subset\Sh(X;\mathcal{E})

the full subcategory spanned by constructible sheaves.

Definition 4.0.7
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space and let
\mathcal{E}\in\Cat_{\infty}
.
We let

\Cons_{P,\omega}(X;\mathcal{E})\subset\Cons_{P}(X;\mathcal{E})

be the full subcategory spanned by constructible sheaves with compact stalks.

Recollection 4.0.8
.

[
HPT24
, Corollary 4.1.15]

Let
(X,P)
be a conically refineable stratified space and let
\Pi_{\infty}(X,P)
be the opposite of the full subcategory of objects
x\in\Cons_{P}(X;\Spc)
such that the functor

\Map(x,-)\colon\Cons_{P}(X;\Spc)\to\Spc

commutes with colimits.
Then
[
HPT24
, Corollary 4.1.15]
shows that for every
\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}
, there is a canonical equivalence

\Cons_{P}(X;\mathcal{E})\simeq\Fun(\Pi_{\infty}(X,P),\mathcal{E}).

Example 4.0.9
.

By
[
PT22
, Theorem 5.4.1]
, if
(X,P)
is a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata, then
\Pi_{\infty}(X,P)
agrees with Lurie’s simplicial set of exit paths (see
[
Lur17
, Definition A.6.2 & Theorem A.6.4]
).
By
[
HPT24
, Theorem 0.3.1-(3)]
, if
(X,P)
admits a conical refinement
(X,Q)
with locally weakly contractible strata, then
\Pi_{\infty}(X,P)
is a localization of
\Pi_{\infty}(X,Q)
.
In particular, the objects of
\Pi_{\infty}(X,P)
are exactly the points of
X
.

Definition 4.0.10

(
[
PT22
, Definition 2.2.1]
)
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically refineable stratified space.

(i)

We say that
(X,P)
is categorically compact if
\Pi_{\infty}(X,P)\in\Cat_{\infty}^{\omega}
.

(ii)

We say that
(X,P)
is locally categorically compact if
X
admits a fundamental system of open subsets
\left\{U_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}
such that each
(U_{i},P)
is categorically compact.

Example 4.0.11
.

Real algebraic varieties with finite stratification by Zariski locally closed subsets are conically refineable, categorically compact, locally categorically compact and have locally weakly contractible strata by
[
HPT25
, Theorem 2.4.12 & Remark 2.4.13]
(see also
[
HPT24
, Theorem 5.3.13]
).

Definition 4.0.12
.

Let
X
be a topological space.
We say that a stratification
X\to P
is locally finite if for every point
x\in X
, there is an open neighborhood
U
of
x
such that
U
intersects only finitely many strata of
(X,P)
.

Example 4.0.13
.

Real compact subanalytic spaces with locally finite stratification by subanalytic subsets are conically refineable, categorically compact, locally categorically compact and have locally weakly contractible strata by
[
HPT25
, Theorem 2.4.10 & Remark 2.4.13]
(see also
[
HPT24
, Theorem 5.3.9]
).

Definition 4.0.14
.

A complex subanalytic stratified space is the data of a triple
(M,X,P)
where
M
is a smooth complex analytic space,
X\subset M
is a locally closed complex subanalytic subset, and
X\to P
is a locally finite stratification by complex subanalytic subsets of
M
.

Definition 4.0.15
.

A complex algebraic stratified space is the data of a stratified space
(X,P)
where
X
is (the complex points of) an algebraic variety over
\mathbb{C}
and
X\to P
is a finite stratification by Zariski locally closed subsets.

Definition 4.0.16
.

Let
A
be a smooth manifold.
A stratified space
(X,P)
on a subset
X
of
A
is said to be a Whitney stratified space if the following conditions hold:

(1)

P
is finite;

(2)

for every
p\in P
, the stratum
X_{p}
is smooth;

(3)

each point has a neighborhood intersecting only a finite number of strata;

(4)

if
p\in P
and
\overline{X_{p}}
is the closure of
X_{p}
in
A
, the intersection
(X_{p}\setminus\overline{X_{p}})\cap X
is a union of strata;

(5)

for every
p,q\in P
,
p<q
, the pair
(X_{p},X_{q})
satisfies Whitney’s conditions
B
(see e.g.
[
GM88
, Section 1.2]
).

Theorem 4.0.17

(
[
NV23
, Theorem 1.1 & Proposition 2.2]
)
.

Whitney stratified spaces are conically stratified.

Definition 4.0.18
.

A complex Whitney stratified space is a stratified space
(X,P)
such that:

(1)

(X,P)
is a complex algebraic stratified space or a compact complex subanalytic stratified space;

(2)

(X,P)
is a Whitney stratified space.

If moreover
X
is smooth, we say that
(X,P)
is a complex Whitney stratified manifold.

Theorem 4.0.19
.

Complex Whitney stratified spaces have locally weakly contractible strata and are conically stratified, locally categorically compact and categorically compact.

Complex Whitney stratified spaces are conically stratified by
Theorem

4.0.17
.
By
[
HPT25
, Theorem 2.4.10 & Remark 2.4.13]
(see also
[
HPT24
, Theorem 5.3.9]
), complex subanalytic stratified spaces have locally weakly contractible strata and are locally categorically compact, and categorically compact if
X
is compact.
Suppose now that
(X,P)
is a complex algebraic stratified space with
X
affine.
Then
(X,P)
is a complex subanalytic stratified space, hence locally categorically compact with locally weakly contractible strata.
By
[
JLMS22
, Proposition 2.2 & Proposition 2.6]
, we can suppose that
X
is the (real points of) an affine variety over
\mathbb{R}
and
X\to P
is a finite stratification by Zariski locally closed subsets.
In this case
(X,P)
is categorically compact by
[
HPT24
, Theorem 5.3.13]
.
In general, a complex algebraic stratified space
(X,P)
can be covered by affine charts.
Hence
(X,P)
have locally weakly contractible strata and is locally categorically by the above discussion.
Since
X
admits a finite cover by affine subsets whose iterated intersections are again affine, hence categorically compact,
[
HPT24
, Theorem 5.1.7-(5)]
shows that
(X,P)
is categorically compact.
∎

4.1.
Perverse
t
-structures

In this paragraph we recall the definition of perverse
t
-structures and prove that these are admissible (
Lemma

4.1.6
) and satisfy
3.0.1
(
Proposition

4.1.8
).

Definition 4.1.1
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space and
\mathfrak{p}\colon P\to\mathbb{Z}
be a function.
Let
R
be a simplicial commutative ring.
Consider the pair of full subcategories
\Sh(X;\Mod_{R})
defined by

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Sh(X;\Mod_{R})_{\geq 0}\coloneqq\big\{F\in\Sh(X;\Mod_{R})\mid\ \forall p\in P\ ,\ \pi_{i}(i_{p}^{\ast}(F))=0\text{ for every }i\leq\mathfrak{p}(p)\big\},

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Sh(X;\Mod_{R})_{\leq 0}\coloneqq\big\{F\in\Sh(X;\Mod_{R})\mid\ \forall p\in P\ ,\ \pi_{i}(i_{p}^{!}(F))=0\text{ for every }i\geq\mathfrak{p}(p)\big\}.

The
\infty
-category of perverse sheaves is

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv(X;\Mod_{R})\coloneqq{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Sh(X;\Mod_{R})_{\leq 0}\cap{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Sh(X;\Mod_{R})_{\geq 0}.

Definition 4.1.2
.

In the setting of
Definition

4.1.1
, we set

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Cons_{P}(X;\Mod_{R})_{\geq 0}\coloneqq{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Sh(X;\Mod_{R})_{\geq 0}\cap\Cons_{P}(X;\Mod_{R})\subset\Cons_{P}(X;\Mod_{R}).

We define analogously
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Cons_{P}(X;\Mod_{R})_{\leq 0}
,
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P}(X;\Mod_{R})
,
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Cons_{P,\omega}(X;\Mod_{R})_{\geq 0}
,
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Cons_{P,\omega}(X;\Mod_{R})_{\leq 0}
,
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P,\omega}(X;\Mod_{R})
.

Recollection 4.1.3
.

Let
(X,P)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata and let
S\subset P
be a closed subset and let
U\coloneqq P\setminus S
.
Consider the corresponding open and closed immersions
j\colon X_{U}\hookrightarrow X
and
i\colon X_{S}\to X
.
Let
R
be a simplicial commutative ring.
By
[
Hai25
, Corollary 2.18 & Proposition 2.26]
we have a recollement of
\infty
-categories

i_{\ast}\colon\Sh(X_{S};\Mod_{R})\rightarrow\Sh_{P}(X;\Mod_{R})\leftarrow\Sh(X_{U};\Mod_{R})\colon j_{\ast}

By
[
PT22
, Corollary 6.8.2]
, the above restricts to a recollement of
\infty
-categories

i_{\ast}\colon\Cons_{S}(X_{S};\Mod_{R})\rightarrow\Cons_{P}(X;\Mod_{R})\leftarrow\Cons_{U}(X_{U};\Mod_{R})\colon j_{\ast}

and, if furthermore
(X,P)
is locally categorically compact, then the above further restricts to a recollement

i_{\ast}\colon\Cons_{S,\omega}(X_{S};\Mod_{R})\rightarrow\Cons_{P,\omega}(X;\Mod_{R})\leftarrow\Cons_{U,\omega}(X_{U};\Mod_{R})\colon j_{\ast}

Recollection 4.1.4
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a stable
\infty
-category and let

D_{F}\xhookrightarrow{i_{\ast}}\mathcal{D}\xhookleftarrow{j_{\ast}}D_{U}

be a pair of full subcategories, respectively equipped with
t
-structures
\tau_{F},\tau_{U}
.
Suppose that
\mathcal{D}
is a recollement of
\mathcal{D}_{U}
and
D_{F}
, so that there are adjunctions
i^{\ast}\dashv i_{\ast}\dashv i^{!}
and
j^{\ast}\dashv j_{\ast}
. Since the notion of
t
-structure depends only on the underlying homotopy category, it follows from
[
BBDG18
, Theorem 4.1.10]
that the pair of full subcategories

\mathcal{D}_{\geq 0}\coloneqq\{d\in\mathcal{D}\mid\ j^{\ast}d\in(\mathcal{D}_{U})_{\geq 0},i^{\ast}d\in(\mathcal{D}_{F})_{\geq 0}\},

\mathcal{D}_{\leq 0}\coloneqq\{d\in\mathcal{D}\mid\ j^{\ast}d\in(\mathcal{D}_{U})_{\leq 0},i^{!}d\in(\mathcal{D}_{F})_{\leq 0}\},

define a
t
-structure on
\mathcal{D}
.

Proposition 4.1.5
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space with
P
finite and let
\mathfrak{p}\colon P\to\mathbb{Z}
be a function.

(1)

The pair of
\infty
-categories
({}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Sh(X;\Mod_{R})_{\leq 0},{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Sh(X;\Mod_{R})_{\geq 0})
of
Definition

4.1.1
define a
t
-structure
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau
on
\Sh(X;\Mod_{R})
.

(2)

If
(X,P)
is conically stratified with locally weakly constructible strata, the
t
-structure
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau
restricts to a
t
-structure on
\Cons_{P}(X;\Mod_{R})
.

(3)

If
(X,P)
is conically stratified with locally weakly constructible strata and locally categorically compact, and if
R
is a discrete regular noetherian ring, the
t
-structure
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau
restricts to a
t
-structure on
\Cons_{P,\omega}(X;\Mod_{R})
.

Point
(1)
is proven in
[
HPT25
, Lemma 3.2.2]
, where it is shown that the pair of
\infty
-categories
({}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Sh(X;\Mod_{R})_{\leq 0},{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Sh(X;\Mod_{R})_{\geq 0})
is the
t
-structure obtained by successive recollement of the shifted standard
t
-structure on
\Sh(X_{p};\Mod_{R})
,
p\in P
.
Point
(2)
then follows by
4.1.3
and the fact that the standard
t
-structure on
\Sh(X_{p};\Mod_{R})
restricts to a
t
-structure on
\Loc(X_{p};\Mod_{R})
,
p\in P
.
If
R
is a discrete regular noetherian, the
t
-structure on
\Mod_{R}
restricts to a
t
-structure on
\Mod_{R}^{\omega}=\Perf(R)
by
[
Sta25
,
Proposition 07LT
]
and
[
Sta25
,
Lemma 066Z
]
.
Hence the standard
t
-structure on
\Loc(X_{p};\Mod_{R})
restricts to a
t
-structure on
\Loc_{\omega}(X_{p};\Mod_{R})
,
p\in P
.
Point
(3)
then follows again by
4.1.3
.
∎

Lemma 4.1.6
.

Let
(X,P)
be a locally categorically compact conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata.
Let
\mathfrak{p}\colon P\to\mathbb{Z}
be a function and let
A\in\dAff_{k}
.
Then the perverse
t
-structure on
\Cons_{P}(X;\Mod_{A})
is
\omega
-accessible and non-degenerate.

We begin by proving that
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau
is
\omega
-accessible.
Let
p\in P
.
Our assumptions guarantee that
i_{p}^{!}\colon\Cons_{P}(X;\Mod_{k})\to\Loc(X_{p};\Mod_{k})
commutes with filtered colimits (
[
PT22
, Proposition 6.9.2]
).
Moreover the standard
t
-structure on
\Loc(X_{p};\Mod_{k})
is clearly
\omega
-accessible.
In particular, for every filtered diagram
\left\{F_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}\in\Cons_{P}(X;\Mod_{k})
and every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
we have equivalences

\pi_{n}(i_{p}^{!}\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i\in I}F_{i})\simeq\pi_{n}(\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i\in I}i_{p}^{!}F_{i})\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{i\in I}\pi_{n}(i_{p}^{!}F_{i}).

The result then follows by definition of
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Cons_{P}(X;\Mod_{k})_{\leq 0}
.

We now prove that
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau
is non-degenerate. Again by
[
PT22
, Lemma 7.3.9]
we can assume that
P
is finite.
Since the standard
t
-structure on locally constant sheaves is clearly left-complete, it follows that the perverse
t
-structure is also left-complete.
Moreover, the argument at the bottom of page 56 of
[
BBDG18
]
implies that the perverse
t
-structure is also right-complete, thus non-degenerate.
∎

Notation 4.1.7
.

Let
(Y,Q)
be stratified spaces.
Let
A\in\dAff_{k}
.
Given a functor

f\colon\Cons_{Q}(Y;\Mod_{A})\to\Cons_{P}(X;\Mod_{A}),

we set

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}f\coloneqq{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\pi_{0}\circ f\colon\Cons_{Q}(Y;\Mod_{A})\to{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P}(X;\Mod_{A}).

Proposition 4.1.8
.

Let
R
be a regular noetherian ring.
Let
(X,P)
be a locally categorically compact conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata and
P
finite.
Let
F\in{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P,\omega}(X)
.
Then every subobject of
F
lies in
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P,\omega}(X)
.

Since perfect complexes satisfy the two-out-of-three properties, it is equivalent to show that the quotients of
F
lie in
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P,\omega}(X)
.
Up to replace
X
by an open subset, we can suppose that
(X,P)
is categorically compact conical with locally weakly contractible strata.
In particular,
\Pi_{\infty}(X,P)
admits a finite number of objects up to equivalence.
We argue by induction on the cardinality of
P
. If
P=\left\{\ast\right\}
, the category of perverse sheaves is up to a shift identified with
\Loc(X;\Mod_{R}^{\heartsuit})
.
In this case,
Proposition

4.1.8
boils down to the fact that a quotient of a finitely presented module over a noetherian ring is again finitely presented. We now proceed with the inductive step.

Let
G
be a quotient of
F
.
Consider a maximal element
p\in P
and let
j\colon X_{p}\hookrightarrow X
and
i\colon X\setminus X_{p}\hookrightarrow X
be the inclusions.
The localization sequence
[
BBDG18
, Lemma 1.4.19]
attached to the open subset
X_{p}\subset X
gives rise to a morphism of exact sequences of perverse sheaves

(4.1.9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{i_{\ast}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\pi_{1}(i^{\ast}F)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}j_{!}j^{\ast}F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{i_{\ast}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}i^{\ast}F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{i_{\ast}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\pi_{1}(i^{\ast}G)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}j_{!}j^{\ast}G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{i_{\ast}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}i^{\ast}G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{2}}

Since
R
is regular noetherian,
Proposition

4.1.5
implies that the perverse sheaves in the top row of (
4.1.9
) have perfect stalks.
Since
F\to G
and
G\to i_{\ast}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}i^{\ast}G
are epimorphisms, so is the right vertical arrow.
Applying the inductive hypothesis we get that
i_{\ast}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}i^{\ast}G
has perfect stalks.
Since
j^{\ast}
is
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau
-exact, it preserves epimorphisms.
In particular
j^{\ast}F\to j^{\ast}G
is an epimorphism.
Hence,
j^{\ast}G
has perfect stalks by the unstratified case already treated.
By
Proposition

4.1.5
, we deduce that
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}j_{!}j^{\ast}G
has perfect stalks.
To conclude, we are left to show that
i_{\ast}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\pi_{1}(i^{\ast}G)
has perfect stalks.
By
[
BBDG18
, 1.4.17.1]
, we know that
i_{\ast}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\pi_{1}(i^{\ast}G)
is a subobject of
i_{\ast}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}i^{!}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}j_{!}j^{\ast}G
.
Since
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}j_{!}j^{\ast}G
has perfect stalks, it follows from
Proposition

4.1.5
that
i_{\ast}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}i^{!}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}j_{!}j^{\ast}G
has perfect stalks.
Hence
i_{\ast}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\pi_{1}(i^{\ast}G)
has perfect stalks by our inductive hypothesis.
∎

Example 4.1.10
.

By
Theorem

4.0.19
, complex Whitney stratified space satisfy the assumptions of
Proposition

4.1.8
.

Example 4.1.11
.

By
Example

4.0.11
,
Example

4.0.13
and
Theorem

4.0.17
, real algebraic varieties and real compact analytic spaces equipped with Whitney stratification satisfy the assumptions of
Proposition

4.1.8
.

4.2.
Existence of good moduli spaces for
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)

In this subsection we specialize the results of
Section

3.3
to moduli spaces of perverse sheaves.
We also prove that for complex Whitney stratified manifolds, the entire algebraic stack of perverse sheaves admits a good moduli space.

Recollection 4.2.1
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space and
\mathfrak{p}\colon X\to P
be a function.
The derived prestack of constructible sheaves is defined by the assignment

\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)\colon\dAff_{k}\to\mathrm{Spc}

\Spec(A)\mapsto Cons_{P,\omega}(X;\Mod_{A})^{\simeq}

with functoriality given by extension of scalars.
If
P
is finite, we let

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)\subset\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)

the sub-prestack over
k
spanned by those constructible sheaves that are
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau
-flat as objects of
\Sh(X;\Mod_{R})
.
We will refer to
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
as the derived prestack of perverse sheaves.

Remark 4.2.2
.

If the perverse
t
-structure restricts to a
t
-structure on the
\infty
-category of constructible sheaves (see e.g.
Proposition

4.1.5
), then a constructible sheaf is
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau
-flat as a sheaf if and only if it is so as a constructible sheaf.

Theorem 4.2.3
.

Let
(X,R)
be a conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata, let
\phi\colon R\to P
be a map of posets, let
\mathfrak{p}\colon P\to\mathbb{Z}
be a function.
Assume that
(X,R)
is categorically compact.
Then:

(1)

\Cons_{P}(X;\Mod_{k})\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
is of finite type.

(2)

There is a canonical equivalence

\mathcal{M}_{\Cons_{P}(X;\Mod_{k})}\simeq\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X).

In particular
\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)
is a locally geometric derived stack, locally of finite presentation over
k
.

(3)

If furthermore
(X,R)
is locally categorically compact and
R,P
are finite posets, then the morphism of derived stacks

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)\hookrightarrow\mathbf{Cons}_{P}(X)

is representable by an open immersion.
In particular
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
is a
1
-Artin stack locally of finite presentation over
k
.

This is
[
HPT25
, Lemma 4.1.12, Lemma 4.1.14 & Theorem 4.2.10]
.
∎

For
\Spec(\kappa)\to\Spec(k)
a closed point with
\kappa
algebraically closed, we have the following

Theorem 4.2.4
.

Let
(X,P)
be a categorically compact, locally categorically compact conically stratified space with locally weakly contractible strata and
P
finite.
Then the algebraic
t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
is
\Theta
-reductive and
\mathrm{S}
-complete.
In particular, any closed quasi-compact substack
\mathcal{X}\subset t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
admits a separated good moduli space
X
whose
\kappa
-points parametrize semisimple perverse sheaves with perfect stalks lying over
\mathcal{X}
.
Moreover
X
admits a natural derived enhancement if
\mathcal{X}
is the truncation of a substack of
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
.

By
Lemma

4.1.6
,
Proposition

4.1.8
and
Theorem

4.2.3
, the algebraic stack
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
satisfies the assumptions of
Theorem

3.3.2
.
∎

Example 4.2.5
.

By
Theorem

4.0.19
, complex Whitney stratified spaces satisfy the assumptions of
Theorem

4.2.4
.

Example 4.2.6
.

By
Example

4.0.11
,
Example

4.0.13
and
Theorem

4.0.17
, real algebraic varieties and real compact analytic varieties equipped with Whitney stratification satisfy the assumptions of
Theorem

4.2.4
.

4.2.1.
Good moduli for perverse sheaves on complex Whitney stratified manifolds

In this paragraph we construct a good moduli space for the entire algebraic stack perverse sheaves for the middle perversity on a complex Whitney stratified manifolds.

Remark 4.2.7
.

Notice that
Theorem

3.3.14
provide a class closed substacks of the stack of perverse sheaves admitting a good moduli space.
Roughly, these closed substacks parametrize perverse sheaves that can be written as extension of a fixed finite set of semisimple perverse sheaves.
The good moduli spaces arising in this way are much smaller than the good moduli of perverse sheaves constructed in
Theorem

4.2.32
and define closed algebraic subspace of it by
[
Alp13
, Lemma 4.14]
.

Definition 4.2.8
.

Let
(X,P)
be a complex Whitney stratified space.
The middle perversity is the function

\mathfrak{p}\colon P\to\mathbb{Z}

p\mapsto\dim_{\mathbb{C}}X_{p}\ .

Definition 4.2.9
.

For
K
be a field,
P\in\Perf(K)
and
i\in\mathbb{N}
, we set

h_{i}(P)\coloneqq\dim_{K}\pi_{i}(P),\qquad\chi(F)\coloneqq\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}(-1)^{j}h_{j}(P).

Definition 4.2.10
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Let
\mathbf{Perf}_{k}^{\left\{\chi=n\right\}}\subset\mathbf{Perf}_{k}
be the sub-presheaf

\mathbf{Perf}_{k}^{\left\{\chi=n\right\}}\colon\dAff_{k}^{op}\to\mathrm{Spc}

defined by sending
\Spec(A)\in\dAff_{k}
to the maximal
\infty
-subgroupoid of
\Perf(A)
spanned by those perfect complexes
F
for which the following property holds: for every field
K
and every
A\to\pi_{0}(A)\to K
we have
\chi(F\otimes_{A}K)=n
.

Lemma 4.2.11
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
The the inclusion

\mathbf{Perf}_{k}^{\left\{\chi=n\right\}}\subset\mathbf{Perf}_{k}

is representable by an open and closed immersion.
In particular, the presheaf
\mathbf{Perf}_{k}^{\left\{\chi=n\right\}}
is a locally geometric derived stack, locally of finite presentation over
k
.

The first claim follows from locally constancy of Euler characteristic (
[
Sta25
,
Lemma 0BDJ
]
).
Since
\mathbf{Perf}_{k}
is a derived stack over
k
, locally geometric and locally of finite presentation over
k
by
[
TV07
, Proposition 3.7]
, the second claim follows the first.
∎

Definition 4.2.12
.

Let
X
be a topological space and
f\colon X\to\mathbb{Z}
.
We say that
f
is constructible if there exists a stratification
(X,P)
of
X
such that
f
is constant on the strata of
(X,P)
.

Definition 4.2.13
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space and
K
a field.
For
F\in\Cons_{P,\omega}(X;\Mod_{K})
, define the local Euler-Poincaré index of
F
by

\chi(F)\colon X\to\mathbb{Z}

x\mapsto\chi(F_{x}).

Definition 4.2.14
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space and
\chi\colon X\to\mathbb{Z}
be a constructible function.
Let
K
be a field.
We denote by

\Cons_{P,\omega}^{\chi}(X,\Mod_{K})\subset\Cons_{P,\omega}(X,\Mod_{K})

the full subcategory spanned by those constructible sheaves
F\in\Cons_{P,\omega}(X,\Mod_{K})
for which
\chi(F)=\chi
.

Definition 4.2.15
.

In the setting of
Definition

4.2.14
, let
\mathfrak{p}\colon P\to\mathbb{Z}
be a function.
We define

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P,\omega}^{\chi}(X,\Mod_{K})\coloneqq{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P,\omega}(X,\Mod_{K})\cap\Cons_{P,\omega}^{\chi}(X,\Mod_{K})\subset{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P,\omega}(X,\Mod_{K}).

Definition 4.2.16
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space admitting a categorically compact conical refinement.
Consider a finite set of representatives
\left\{x_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}
for the equivalence classes of
\Pi_{\infty}(X,P)
.
Let
\nu\colon\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{N}
be function and
K
a field.
We denote by

\Cons_{P,\omega}^{\nu}(X,\Mod_{K})\subset\Cons_{P,\omega}(X,\Mod_{K})

the full subcategory spanned by those constructible sheaves
F
with perfect stalks for which
\sum_{i\in I}h^{j}(F_{x_{i}})\leq\nu(j)
for every
j\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Definition 4.2.17
.

In the setting of
Definition

4.2.16
, let
\mathfrak{p}\colon P\to\mathbb{Z}
be a function.
We define

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P,\omega}^{\nu}(X,\Mod_{K})\coloneqq{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P,\omega}(X,\Mod_{K})\cap\Cons_{P,\omega}^{\nu}(X,\Mod_{K})\subset{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P,\omega}(X,\Mod_{K}).

Remark 4.2.18
.

By
4.0.8
and
Example

4.0.9
,
Definition

4.2.16
and
Definition

4.2.17
do not depend on the choice of representatives for the equivalence classes of
\Pi_{\infty}(X,P)
.

Remark 4.2.19
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space with
X
an analytic manifold and let
F\in\Cons_{P,\omega}(X;\Mod_{\mathbb{C}})
.
Attached to
F
there is a lagrangian cycle
CC(F)\subset T^{\ast}(X)
, the characteristic cycle of
F
, which roughly measure how far is
F
from being locally constant.
In this paragraph, we will need to invoke the theory of characteristic cycles.
We will blackbox it and refer to
[
KS90
, Chapter IX]
for an introductory exposition.

Remark 4.2.20
.

Let
(X,P)
be a Whitney stratified manifold and
\mathfrak{p}
the middle perversity.
Let
K
be a field and
F\in\Perv_{P}(X;\Mod_{K})
.
Then for every
x\in X
we have
h_{i}(F_{x})=0
for every
i<0
and
i>\dim_{\mathbb{C}}X
.
Indeed for
i<0
this follows directly by the definition of the connective part of
\Perv_{P}(X;\Mod_{K})
.
For
i>\dim_{\mathbb{C}}X
, see the argument given in
[
BBDG18
]
, bottom of page 56.

Theorem 4.2.21

(Massey)
.

Let
(X,P)
be a Whitney stratified manifold and let
\mathfrak{p}\colon P\to\mathbb{Z}
be the middle perversity.
Let
\chi\colon X\to\mathbb{Z}
be a constructible function.
There exists a function
\nu\colon\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{N}
with finite support such that

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}Perv_{P,\omega}^{\chi}(X;\Mod_{\mathbb{C}})\subset{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P,\omega}^{\nu}(X;\mathbb{C}).

By
Remark

4.2.20
, it is enough to show that a choice of
\chi
bounds the dimensions of the cohomologies
h_{i}
of the stalks in the range
0\leq i\leq\dim_{\mathbb{C}}X
.
By
[
KS90
, Theorem 9.7.11]
, fixing
\chi
is equivalent to fixing the characteristic cycle.
Up to taking an open neighborhood of
X
, we can assume
X\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}
.
Up to shrinking again
X
,
[
Mas94
, Proposition 2.7]
shows that the hypothesis of
[
Mas94
, Corollary 5.5 & Theorem 7.5]
are satisfied.
Together with
Remark

4.2.20
, this shows that a choice of
\chi
bounds the dimension of the cohomologies of the stalks, hence proving the claim.
∎

Definition 4.2.22
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space admitting a categorically compact, locally categorically compact conical refinement with locally weakly contractible strata and let
\mathfrak{p}\colon P\to\mathbb{Z}
be a function.
Let
\chi\colon X\to\mathbb{Z}
be a constructible function.
We define the sub-presheaf of
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\chi}(X)\colon\dAff_{k}^{op}\to\mathrm{Spc}

by sending
\Spec(A)\in\dAff_{k}
to the maximal
\infty
-subgrupoid of
\Cons_{P,\omega}(X;\Mod_{A})
spanned by those objects
F
for which the following property holds:
F
is
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau
-flat over
A
and for every field
K
and every
A\to\pi_{0}(A)\to K
we have
\chi(F_{x}\otimes_{A}K)=\chi(x)
for every
x\in X
.

Definition 4.2.23
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space.
Consider a set of representants
\left\{x_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}
of the equivalence classes of
\Pi_{\infty}(X,P)
and a set of integers
\left\{\chi_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}
.
These data determine a constructible function

\underline{\chi}\colon X\to\mathbb{Z}

x\mapsto\chi_{i}\ \ \textit{if $[x]=[x_{i}]$ in $\pi_{0}(\Pi_{\infty}(X,P))$.}

Remark 4.2.24
.

If
(X,P)
is a conically refineable stratified space, it follows by
4.0.8
and
Example

4.0.9
that the constructible function
\underline{\chi}
of
Definition

4.2.23
do not depend on the choice of the
x_{i}
’s, but only on the choice of the
\chi_{i}
’s.

Lemma 4.2.25
.

In the setting of
Definition

4.2.23
, suppose that
(X,P)
is conically refineable.
Then we have a pullback square of derived prestack over
k

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\underline{\chi}}(X)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{i\in I}\mathbf{Perf}_{k}^{\left\{\chi=\chi_{i}\right\}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\prod_{i\in I}\mathbf{Perf}_{k}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{i\in I}i^{\ast}_{x_{i}}}

By
4.0.8
and
Example

4.0.9
, the local Euler-Poincaré index is constant on the equivalent classes of
\Pi_{\infty}(X,P)
.
Hence the presheaf
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\underline{\chi}}(X)
can be described as follows.
For
\Spec(A)\in\dAff_{k}
, we have that
F\in{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\underline{\chi}}(X)(\Spec(A))
if and only if the following property holds:
F\in\Cons_{P,\omega}(X;\Mod_{A})
is
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau_{A}
-flat and for every field
K
and every
A\to\pi_{0}(A)\to K
we have
\chi(F_{x_{i}}\otimes_{A}K)=\chi_{i}
for every
i\in I
.
The claim follows.
∎

Lemma 4.2.26
.

In the setting of
Definition

4.2.23
, let
(X,P)
be a stratified space admitting a categorically compact, locally compact refinement with locally weakly contractible strata.
Then the structural morphism

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\underline{\chi}}(X)\to{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)

is representable by an open and closed immersion.
In particular, the presheaf
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\underline{\chi}}(X)
is a
1
-Artin stack locally of finite presentation over
k
.

By
[
HPT24
, Theorem 0.3.1-(5)]
, the stratified space
(X,P)
is categorically compact.
Hence the
\infty
-category
\Pi_{\infty}(X,P)
has a finite number of equivalence classes.
It then follows by
Lemma

4.2.11
and
Lemma

4.2.25
that the structural morphism
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\underline{\chi}}(X)\to{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
is (the pullback of) an open and closed immersion.
The last statement follows since
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
is a
1
-Artin stack locally of finite presentation over
k
by
Theorem

4.2.3
.
∎

Example 4.2.27
.

By
Theorem

4.0.19
,
Lemma

4.2.26
applies to complex Whitney stratified spaces.

Recollection 4.2.28
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space admitting a categorically compact conical refinement
(X,Q)
with locally weakly contractible strata.
By
[
HPT24
, Theorem 0.3.1-(5)]
, the stratified space
(X,P)
is categorically compact.
Denote by
I
the finite set of equivalence classes of
\Pi_{\infty}(X,P)
and choose a point
x_{i}
for every
i\in I
.
Let
A\in\dAff_{k}
and denote by
j_{x_{i}}\colon\left\{x_{i}\right\}\hookrightarrow X
the inclusions. Consider the adjunction

j_{x_{i},\#}\colon\Mod_{A}\rightleftarrows\Cons_{P}(X;\Mod_{A})\colon j_{x_{i}}^{\ast}.

It follows from
[
PT22
, Corollary 6.5.4]
and
[
HPT24
, Theorem 5.1.7-(2)]
that
\bigoplus_{i\in I}j_{x_{i},\#}A
is a compact generator of
\Cons_{P}(X;\Mod_{A})
.

Recollection 4.2.29
.

Let
(X,P)
be a stratified space admitting a categorically compact, locally categorically compact conical refinement with locally weakly contractible strata and let
\mathfrak{p}\colon P\to\mathbb{Z}
be a function.
Consider a finite set of representatives
\left\{x_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}
for the equivalence classes of
\Pi_{\infty}(X,P)
and the associated compact generator
\bigoplus_{i\in I}j_{x_{i},\#}k
of
\Cons_{P}(X;\Mod_{k})
(
4.2.28
).
Let
\nu\colon\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{N}
be a function with finite support.
Consider the algebraic stack
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\nu}(X)
of

3.2.9
and let
\Spec(A)\in\dAff_{k}
.
Unwinding the definitions, we have that
F\in{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\nu}(X)
if and only if the following property holds:
F\in\Cons_{P,\omega}(X;\Mod_{A})
is
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau_{A}
-flat and for every field
K
and every
A\to\pi_{0}(A)\to K
we have
\sum_{i\in I}h_{j}(F_{x_{i}}\otimes_{A}K)\leq\nu(j)
for every
j\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proposition 4.2.30
.

In the setting of
Definition

4.2.23
, let
(X,P)
be a Whitney stratified manifold and
\mathfrak{p}\colon P\to\mathbb{Z}
the middle perversity.
There exists a function
\nu\colon\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{N}
with finite support such that

{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\underline{\chi}}(X)\subset{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\nu}(X).

In particular, the algebraic stack
t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\underline{\chi}}(X)
is of finite presentation over
k
.

Let us assume for the moment that the first claim holds.
By
Lemma

4.2.26
, the algebraic stack
t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\underline{\chi}}(X)
is locally of finite presentation over
k
.
Hence we are reduced to prove that
t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\underline{\chi}}(X)
is quasi-compact.
Since by
3.2.9
the algebraic stack
t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\nu}(X)
is of finite presentation over the noetherian ring
k
, it is noetherian.
In particular,
t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\underline{\chi}}(X)
is an open substack of a Noetherian algebraic stack.
Since quasi-compactness of an algebraic stack is a topological property by
[
Sta25
,
Lemma 04YC
]
, it follows that
t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\underline{\chi}}(X)
quasi-compact.

We now show the first claim.
Let
\Spec(A)\in\dAff_{k}
and consider a morphism
\Spec(A)\to{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\underline{\chi}}(X)
corresponding to a
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\tau_{A}
-flat constructible sheaf
F\in\Cons_{P,\omega}(X;\Mod_{A})
.
Consider a morphism
A\to\pi_{0}(A)\to K
with
K
a field.
Since
k
contains
\mathbb{Q}
, we can write
K
as a filtered colimits of its sub-fields
K_{j}
that are finitely generated over
\mathbb{Q}
.
Since
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\underline{\chi}}(X)
is locally of finite presentation, the composition

\Spec(K)\to\Spec(A)\to{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\underline{\chi}}(X)

factors through
\Spec(K_{j})
for some
j
.
Hence we can assume that
K
is finitely generated over
\mathbb{Q}
.
Up to choosing an embedding
K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
, we can assume that
K=\mathbb{C}
.
The claim then follows directly by
Theorem

4.2.21
.
∎

Corollary 4.2.31
.

In the setting of
Proposition

4.2.30
, the connected components of
t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\underline{\chi}}(X)
are quasi-compact.

Keeping the notations of
Definition

4.2.23
, we have a decomposition in open and closed substacks

t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)=\bigsqcup_{\underline{\chi}}t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\underline{\chi}}(X).

In particular, every connected component is a closed substack of
t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}^{\underline{\chi}}(X)
for some
\underline{\chi}
.
The claim then follows by
Proposition

4.2.30
.
∎

For
\Spec(\kappa)\to\Spec(k)
a closed point with
\kappa
algebraically closed, we have the following

Theorem 4.2.32
.

Let
(X,P)
be a Whitney stratified manifold and
\mathfrak{p}\colon P\to\mathbb{Z}
the middle perversity.
The algebraic stack
t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
admits a separated good moduli space
t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P}(X)
.
Moreover, the
\kappa
-points of
t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P}(X)
parametrize semisimple perverse sheaves with perfect stalks.
Furthermore,
t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P}(X)
admits a natural derived enhancement
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\Perv_{P}(X)
which is a derived good moduli space for
{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
.

By
Theorem

4.2.3
,
Lemma

4.1.6
,
Proposition

4.1.8
,
Theorem

4.0.19
and
Corollary

4.2.31
, the algebraic stack
t_{0}{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\mathbf{Perv}_{P}(X)
satisfies the assumptions of
Corollary

3.3.3
.
∎

Appendix A

In this Appendix we give a description of the derived
\infty
-category of quasi-coherent sheaves on
\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}
.
We recall that
\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}
is defined as

\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\coloneqq\left[{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\displaystyle{\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\displaystyle{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}$}}{\raisebox{3.75pt}{$\textstyle{\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-3.41666pt}{$\textstyle{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\scriptstyle{\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-2.39166pt}{$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}$}}{\raisebox{3.41666pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))}$}\mkern-5.0mu\diagup\mkern-4.0mu\raisebox{-1.6994pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}$}}}\right],

where
R
is a DVR,
\pi
a uniformizer for
R
and the action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
is induced by the one on
\mathbb{A}^{2}
(where
s
has weight
1
and
t
has weight
-1
).
The statements in
3.2.1
are of course highly inspired by
[
AHLH23
, Lemma 7.14]
, and they summarize all the contents of this Appendix.
The proof is instead inspired
[
Mou21
]
, where the equivalent statements about
\QCoh([\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}])
are proven.

A.1.
A description of
\QCoh(\Theta)

We recall the main results of
[
Mou21
]
for reader’s convenience.
The results are stated over the sphere spectrum
\mathbb{S}
, but they hold for any
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring spectrum.
We denote by
\mathbb{Z}
the poset of integers and by
\mathbb{Z}^{ds}
the underlying set.
In the following we use the convention introduced in
3.1.1
.

Theorem A.1.1
.

(1)

(
[
Mou21
, Theorem 4.1]
&
[
Lur14
, Proposition 3.1.6]
)
There is an equivalence

\QCoh(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m})\simeq\Rep(\mathbb{Z})\simeq\mathrm{CoMod}_{\mathbb{S}[\mathbb{Z}]}

of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories.

(2)

(
[
Mou21
, Theorem 1.1]
) We have an equivalence of symmetric monoidal categories

\QCoh([\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}])\simeq\Mod_{\mathbb{S}[x]}(\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds}))\simeq\Rep(\mathbb{Z})\ .

(3)

(
[
Mou21
, Proposition 6.3]
) The pullback along the open immersion
\Spec(\mathbb{S})=[\mathbb{G}_{m}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]\xhookrightarrow{j}[\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
induces the functor

\operatornamewithlimits{colim}\colon\Rep(\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{Sp}

(\cdots\xrightarrow{x}F_{n}\xrightarrow{x}F_{n-1}\xrightarrow{x}\cdots)\mapsto\operatornamewithlimits{colim}(\cdots\xrightarrow{x}F_{n}\xrightarrow{x}F_{n-1}\xrightarrow{x}\cdots)

under the equivalence of point (2).

(4)

(
[
Mou21
, Proposition 6.5]
) The pullback along the closed immersion
\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m}=[\Spec(\mathbb{S})/\mathbb{G}_{m}]\xhookrightarrow{i}[\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
induces the functor

\Gr\colon\Rep(\mathbb{Z})\to\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds})

(\cdots\xrightarrow{x}F_{n}\xrightarrow{x}F_{n-1}\xrightarrow{x}\cdots)\mapsto\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\cof(F_{n}\xrightarrow{x}F_{n-1})

under the equivalence of points (1) and (2).

(5)

(
[
Mou21
, Proposition 8.2]
) The natural
t
-structure on
\QCoh([\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}])
corresponds via the equivalence of point (2) to the standard
t
-structure on
\Rep(\mathbb{Z})
.
That is,
F_{\bullet}\in\Rep(\mathbb{Z})
is (co)connective if and only if
F_{n}
is (co)connective for every
n
.

Remark A.1.2
.

Let
q\colon\Spec(\mathbb{S})\to\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
p\colon\mathbb{A}^{1}\to[\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathrm{G}_{m}]
be the atlas maps.
Via the equivalences of
Theorem

A.1.1
-(1)-(2), there are commutative diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\QCoh(\mathrm{B\mathrm{G}_{m}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\QCoh(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{\mathbb{S}[x]}(\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Sp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\QCoh(\mathbb{A}^{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{\mathbb{S}[x]}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\pi}_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the right vertical arrow is given by (respectively, induced by)

\pi_{!}\colon\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds})\to\mathrm{Sp}

F_{\bullet}\mapsto\oplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}F_{n}\ .

Notation A.1.3
.

In what follows we will make extensive use of the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{A}^{2}_{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathbb{A}^{2}_{R}/\mathbb{G}_{m,R}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spec(R)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m,R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{1}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

All the squares are pullbacks, the horizontal maps are atlases, the top vertical maps are closed immersions and the bottom vertical maps are the natural projections.

Lemma A.1.4
.

The pushforward
\phi_{\ast}\colon\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})\to\QCoh(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m,R})
induces an equivalence

\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})\simeq\Mod_{\phi_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}}(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m,R})

of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories.

Remark A.1.5
.

The proof is a translation in our setting of the discussion at the beginning of
[
Mou21
, Section 5]
.

By
[
Lur11a
, Proposition 3.2.5]
, it is enough to show that

\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))\to\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}

is a quasi-affine morphism.
By
[
Lur11a
, Remark 3.1.29]
, it is enough to show it after pulling back along atlas of
\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m,R}
.
The conclusion follows from the outer pullback diagram of
A.1.3
.
∎

We now identify the graded
R
-module of
\phi_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
under the equivalence of
Theorem

A.1.1
-(1).
This will identify
\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})
as a category of modules in
\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds};\Mod_{R})
.
We begin by identifying the underlying
R
-module.

Lemma A.1.6
.

The underlying
R
-module of
\phi_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
is
R[s,t]/(st-\pi)
.

Consider the diagram of
A.1.3
.
By
[
Lur11a
, Corollary 3.2.6]
, the Beck-Chevalley transformation
p^{\ast}\phi_{\ast}\to(\phi^{\prime})_{\ast}(p^{\prime})^{\ast}
is an equivalence.
It then follows that the underlying
R
-module of
\phi_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
is identified with

p^{\ast}\phi_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}\simeq(\phi^{\prime})_{\ast}(p^{\prime})^{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}\simeq(\phi^{\prime})_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))}\simeq R[s,t]/(st-\pi),

where the last equivalence follows by
\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))
being affine.
∎

Now we compute the graded structure.
In order to do so, we notice that by
A.1.3
, the graded structure is induced by the one on
\mathbb{A}^{2}
.
Thus it is enough to compute it there and this can be done already at the non-commutative level.

Lemma A.1.7
.

Via the equivalence of
Theorem

A.1.1
-(1) the spectral quasi-coherent sheaf
(\phi_{1})_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}}
is identified with
\mathbb{S}[t,s]
, where
t
has weight
-1
and
s
has weight
1
.

By definition,
\mathbb{S}[t,s]
is the
\mathbb{S}[\mathbb{Z}]
-comodule

\mathbb{S}[\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}]\to\mathbb{S}[\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}]\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{S}[\mathbb{Z}]

arising from the natural map of monoids

\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{Z}

(n,m)\mapsto(n,m,m-n).

The comodule structure of on
\phi_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}}
arises from the action map
\mathbb{A}^{2}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbb{A}^{2}
, which is induced by the same map of monoids.
Thus the equivalence of (
A.1.6
) can be promoted to an equivalence of
\mathbb{S}[\mathbb{Z}]
-comodules as stated.
∎

Corollary A.1.8
.

There is an equivalence

\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})\simeq\Mod_{R[s,t]/(st-\pi)}(\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds};\Mod_{R}))

of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories.

It follows formally from
Lemma

A.1.6
and
Lemma

A.1.7
.
See
[
Mou21
, Theorem 5.1]
, for a detailed proof.
∎

Remark A.1.9
.

Let us describe objects of
\Mod_{R[s,t]/(st-\pi)}(\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds};\Mod_{R}))
informally: we have to give ourselves a graded
R
-modules, with an action of
s
that makes the weight increase by
1
and an action of
t
that makes the weight decrease by
1
.
Moreover, we have the relation
st=\pi=ts
.
Thus we can represent such an object by
