{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{72.32642pt}{-7.61108pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

with the relation that composing
s
with
t
(and vice-versa) is the endomorphism induced by multiplication by
\pi
.
This recovers the description of the abelian category
\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})
provided by
[
AHLH23
, Lemma 7.14]
.
Moreover the proof of
[
Mou21
, Proposition 8.2]
carries on in our setting showing under the equivalence of
Corollary

A.1.8
an object
F_{\bullet}
is (co)connective if and only if
F_{n}
is (co)connective for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Remark A.1.10
.

Let
p\colon\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))\to\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}
be the atlas map.
Via the equivalence of
Corollary

A.1.8
there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{R[s,t]/(st-\pi)}(\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds};\Mod_{R}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\QCoh(\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{R[s,t]/(st-\pi)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where the right vertical arrow is induced by

\pi_{!}\colon\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds})\to\mathrm{Sp}

F_{\bullet}\mapsto\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}F_{n}\ .

Corollary A.1.11
.

Let
\ev_{0}\colon\Mod_{R}(\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds}))\to Mod_{R}
be the evaluation at
0
functor and let
\ev_{0,!}\Mod_{R}\to\Mod_{R}(\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds}))
be its left adjoint.
In the setting of
Remark

A.1.10
, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{R[s,t]/(st-\pi)}(\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds};\Mod_{R}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{R}(\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\QCoh(\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{R[s,t]/(st-\pi)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{p^{\ast}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{!}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes_{R}R[s,t]/(st-\pi)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{0,!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes_{R}R[s,t]/(st-\pi)}

By
Remark

A.1.10
, it is enough to show that the right square commutes. Passing to right adjoints, it is enough to show that the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{R[s,t]/(st-\pi)}(\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds};\Mod_{R}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{R}(\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{R[s,t]/(st-\pi)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{0}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}

commutes, where
U
and
U^{\prime}
are the forgetful functors.
Let
M\in\Mod_{R[s,t]/(st-\pi)}
.
The composition
U^{\prime}\circ\pi^{\ast}(M)
is identified with the diagonal functor
\underline{M}
defined by
n\mapsto U(M)
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{ds}
.
The claim follows by evaluating
\underline{M}
at zero.
∎

A.2.
Operations on
\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})

We now want to describe pullback along the open and closed immersion defined by the loci
\left\{t\neq 0\right\}
,
\left\{s\neq 0\right\}
,
\left\{t=0\right\}
and
\left\{s=0\right\}
of
\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}
.

Proposition A.2.1
.

Let
j_{\left\{s\neq 0\right\}},j_{\left\{t\neq 0\right\}}\colon\Spec(R)\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}
be the open immersions.
We have commutative diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\QCoh(\Spec(R))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\QCoh(\Spec(R))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{R[s,t]/(st-\pi)}(\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{R[s,t]/(st-\pi)}(\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\ast}_{\left\{s\neq 0\right\}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\ast}_{\left\{t\neq 0\right\}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatornamewithlimits{colim}_{t}}

where the vertical arrows are equivalences and the bottom horizontal arrows are defined respectively by

F_{\bullet}\mapsto\operatornamewithlimits{colim}(\cdots\xleftarrow{s}F_{n}\xleftarrow{s}F_{n-1}\xleftarrow{s}\cdots)

F_{\bullet}\mapsto\operatornamewithlimits{colim}(\cdots\xrightarrow{t}F_{n}\xrightarrow{t}F_{n-1}\xrightarrow{t}\cdots)

We prove the commutativity of the second diagram, the other being analogue.
Let
F_{\bullet}\in\Mod_{R[s,t]/(st-\pi)}(\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds}))
and set
\overline{R}\coloneqq R[s,t]/(st-\pi)
.
Then we have

R[t^{\pm 1}]\simeq\overline{R}_{t}\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{colim}(\cdots\overline{R}\xrightarrow{t}\overline{R}\xrightarrow{t}\cdots)

It follows that under the equivalences

\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}|_{\left\{t\neq 0\right\}})\simeq\QCoh([\mathbb{G}_{m,R}/\mathbb{G}_{m,R}])\simeq\QCoh(\Spec(R)),

the pullback
j^{\ast}_{\left\{t\neq 0\right\}}\colon\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})\to\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}|_{\left\{t\neq 0\right\}})
then corresponds to weight
0
part of

F_{\bullet}\otimes_{\overline{R}}\overline{R}_{t}\simeq\operatornamewithlimits{colim}(\cdots F_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{t}F_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{t}\cdots).

The claim follows.
∎

Proposition A.2.2
.

Let
\kappa=R/(\pi)
and let
i_{\left\{s=0\right\}},i_{\left\{t=0\right\}}\colon\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m,\kappa}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}
be the closed immersions.
We have commutative diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\QCoh(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m,\kappa})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\QCoh(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m,\kappa})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{R[s,t]/(st-\pi)}(\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{R[s,t]/(st-\pi)}(\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Mod_{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\ast}_{\left\{s=0\right\}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\ast}_{\left\{t=0\right\}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gr_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gr_{t}}

where the vertical arrows are equivalences and the bottom horizontal arrows are defined respectively by

F_{\bullet}\mapsto[\cdots\xrightarrow{t}F_{n}/sF_{n-1}\xrightarrow{t}F_{n-1}/sF_{n-2}\xrightarrow{t}\cdots]

F_{\bullet}\mapsto[\cdots\xleftarrow{s}F_{n}/tF_{n+1}\xleftarrow{s}F_{n-1}/tF_{n}\xleftarrow{s}\cdots]

We prove the commutativity of the second diagram, the other being analogue.
Let
F_{\bullet}\in\Mod_{R[s,t]/(st-\pi)}(\Rep(\mathbb{Z}^{ds}))
and set
\overline{R}\coloneqq R[s,t]/(st-\pi)
.
Then we have

\kappa[s]\simeq\overline{R}/(t)\simeq\cof(\overline{R}\xrightarrow{t}\overline{R})

It follows that
i^{\ast}_{\left\{s=0\right\}}
corresponds to

F_{\bullet}\otimes_{\overline{R}}\kappa[s]\simeq\cof(F_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{t}F_{\bullet}).

The claim follows.
∎

Consider now the cover of
\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}
given by the inclusion of the open substacks

\Spec(R)\xhookrightarrow{j_{\left\{s\neq 0\right\}}}\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\},

\Spec(R)\xhookrightarrow{j_{\left\{t\neq 0\right\}}}\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}.

These open substacks intersect in the locus

\Spec(K)\xhookrightarrow{j_{\left\{s,t\neq 0\right\}}}\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\},

where
K\coloneqq\Frac(R)
.
Hence we get an equivalence

(A.2.3)

\QCoh(\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\})\simeq\QCoh(\Spec(R))\times_{\QCoh(\Spec(K))}\QCoh(\Spec(R)).

This allows us to prove the following:

Lemma A.2.4
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}
equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
.
Then (
A.2.3
) restricts to an equivalence between
\tau
-flat families over
\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}
and the full
\infty
-category of
\QCoh(\Spec(R))\times_{\QCoh(\Spec(K))}\QCoh(\Spec(R))
spanned by the following data:

(a)

a
\tau
-flat family
F
over
K
;

(b)

\tau
-flat families
E_{1},E_{2}
over
R
equipped with injections
E_{i}\hookrightarrow F
in
\mathcal{C}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
;

(c)

equivalences
E_{i}\otimes_{R}K\simeq F
.

Since
\tau
-flatness satisfies faithfully flat descent (
Lemma

2.2.15
), this follows directly from the covering of
\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}
described above.
More precisely, let
F_{\bullet}
be a
\tau
-flat family over
\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}
.
Then under the equivalence of (
A.2.3
), the family
F
corresponds to
(j_{\left\{s,t\neq 0\right\}})_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet}
, the family
E_{1}
to
(j_{\left\{t\neq 0\right\}})_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet}
and the family
E_{2}
to
(j_{\left\{s\neq 0\right\}})_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet}
.
∎

Corollary A.2.5
.

In the setting of
Lemma

A.2.4
, let
F_{\bullet}
be a
\tau
-flat family over
\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}
and
j\colon\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}\setminus\left\{0\right\}\to\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}
be the open immersion.
Set
E_{1}\simeq(j_{\left\{t\neq 0\right\}})_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet}
and
E_{2}\simeq(j_{\left\{s\neq 0\right\}})^{\ast}F_{\bullet}
.
Then
\pi_{0}j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F_{\bullet}
is identified with
\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}E_{1}\cap(\pi^{-n}E_{2})t^{n}
.

Set
F\simeq(j_{\left\{s,t\neq 0\right\}})_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet}
.
The underlying
R[s,t]/(st-\pi)
-family of
\pi_{0}j_{\ast}F_{\bullet}
is identified with

\mathrm{Ker}(E_{1}\oplus E_{2}\to F).

By means of the equivalence
[\mathbb{G}_{m}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]\simeq\Spec(R)
, the family
E_{1}\in\mathcal{C}_{R}
corresponds to the graded object

E_{1}\otimes_{R}R[t^{\pm 1}]

Similarly for
E_{2}
and
F
.
Hence the pushforward of these families to
\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}
are identified respectively with:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}

Using that
E_{2}\subseteq F
, the last family is equivalent to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi E_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi^{-1}E_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

The pushforward
j_{\mathcal{C},\ast}F_{\bullet}
is then identified with

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi E_{2}\cap E_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{E_{2}\cap E_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi^{-1}E_{2}\cap E_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}

as claimed.
∎

Lemma A.2.6
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\mathrm{Pr}^{\mathrm{L},\omega}_{k}
equipped with an admissible
t
-structure
\tau
.
Let
F_{\bullet}\in\mathcal{C}_{\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
.
If the restrictions
(j_{\left\{s\neq 0\right\}})_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet},(j_{\left\{s\neq 0\right\}})_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet},(i_{\left\{s=t=0\right\}})_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet}
are
\tau
-flat, then
F_{\bullet}
is
\tau
-flat.

By
Lemma

2.2.15
it is enough to show that
p_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet}
is
\tau
-flat.
Consider the pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{G}_{m}=\Spec(R[s^{\pm 1}])}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathbb{G}_{m}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]=\Spec(R)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\left\{s\neq 0\right\}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\left\{s\neq 0\right\}}}

where the vertical maps are the atlas maps and the horizontal are open immersions.
By hypothesis,
(j_{\left\{s\neq 0\right\}})_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet}
is
\tau_{R}
-flat, which in turn implies that
q_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}(j_{\left\{s\neq 0\right\}})_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet}\simeq(j_{\left\{s\neq 0\right\}})_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}p^{\ast}F_{\bullet}
is
\tau_{R[s^{\pm 1}]}
-flat.
Similarly, the restriction of
p_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet}
to the locus
\left\{t\neq 0\right\}
is
\tau_{R[t^{\pm 1}]}
-flat.
Consider now the pullback diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spec(\kappa)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spec(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathrm{ST}}_{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\left\{s=t=0\right\}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\left\{s=t=0\right\}}}

Arguing as above we find that
(i_{\left\{s=t=0\right\}})_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}p_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet}
is
\tau_{\kappa}
-flat.
Hence
p_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}F_{\bullet}
is
\tau
-flat by
Lemma

2.3.5
.
∎
